&\Omega^{2}(u_{0},v)\leq D\cdot e^{2K_{-}v+Cv^{1-\eta}},\\
&(2+\eta)K_{-}\leq-\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})(u_{-},v)\leq(2-\eta)K_{-}<0\\
&L_{-}\ v^{-2s}\leq-r\partial_{v}r(u_{0},v)\leq L_{+}\ v^{-2s},\\
&|\phi|^{2}(u_{0},v),\ |Q|(u_{0},v)\leq D,\end{split}

(3.1)

where
s>\frac{1}{2}
,
\eta\in(0,1)
. Then, assuming
v_{0}
is large enough with respect to the constants involved in (
3.1
),

i.

u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}\in(u_{0},u_{F}]
and
[u_{0},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)\subset\mathcal{T}
. Moreover, there exists
D_{-}>0
,
D_{+}>0
, such that for all
u\in[u_{0},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}]
,
v\geq v_{0}

\begin{split}&D_{-}\ v^{-2s}\leq-r\partial_{v}r(u,v)\leq D_{+}\ v^{-2s},\\
&r^{2}_{CH}(u)+\frac{2D_{-}}{2s-1}\cdot v^{1-2s}\leq r^{2}(u,v)\leq r^{2}_{CH}(u)+\frac{2D_{+}}{2s-1}\cdot v^{1-2s}.\end{split}

(3.2)

If, moreover, there exists
s>1
such that the initial data satisfy (
3.1
) and the additional estimate:

\begin{split}|D_{v}\phi|(u_{0},v)\leq\tilde{D}\cdot v^{-s}\end{split}

(3.3)

for some
\tilde{D}>0
, then the following spacetime estimates are satisfied: for all
u\in[u_{0},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}]
,
v\geq v_{0}
:

\begin{split}&|\phi|(u,v)\lesssim r^{-\frac{1}{2s-1}}(u,v),\\
&r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim r^{-\frac{s}{s-\frac{1}{2}}}(u,v),\\
&r|D_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s},\\
&|Q|(u,v)\leq\check{D},\end{split}

(3.4)

for some
\check{D}>0
.

ii.

If
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{F}
(breakdown assumption), then for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{F}
, there exists
v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)<+\infty
such that

\lim_{v\rightarrow v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)}r(u,v)=0\text{, }\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}r(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v)=0\text{ and }\lim_{u\rightarrow u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}}r_{CH}(u)=0.

(3.5)

If, moreover, there exists
s>1
such that the initial data satisfy (
3.1
), (
3.3
) and the following additional estimate holds: there exists
D_{L}>0
,
D_{C}>0
,
\delta>0
,
\alpha_{\infty}\in\mathbb{R}
such that for all
v\geq v_{0}
:

\begin{split}&|D_{vv}^{2}\phi|(u_{0},v)\leq D_{C}\cdot v^{-s-1},\end{split}

(3.6)

|\Im(e^{iq_{0}\int_{v_{0}}^{v}A_{v}(u_{0},v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}}e^{-i\alpha_{\infty}}D_{v}\phi(u_{0},v))|\leq D_{C}\cdot|\phi|(u_{0},v)\cdot v^{-s-\delta},

(3.7)

\begin{split}&|D_{v}\phi|(u_{0},v)\geq D_{L}\cdot v^{-s}.\end{split}

(3.8)

Then, defining
\mathcal{S}=\{(u,v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)),\ u\in(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{F})\}
, there exists
0<\epsilon<u_{F}-u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
such that

\mathcal{S}\cap(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon)
is spacelike with the following estimate for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon
:

v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)\approx\left(u-u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2s-1}},\ v^{\prime}_{\mathcal{S}}(u)\approx-\left(u-u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}\right)^{-\frac{2s}{2s-1}}.

(3.9)

Moreover, the metric takes the following approximate Kasner form: there exists coordinates
(x,\tau)
so that
\mathcal{S}=\{\tau=0\}
,
\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}=\{\tau=0,\ x=0\}
and
x_{0}\geq 0
small enough so that for all

0\leq x\leq x_{0}
,
\tau\geq 0
:

\begin{split}&g=-(1+\mathcal{E}_{T}(\tau,x))d\tau^{2}+\tau^{2(1-2p(\tau,x))}(1+\mathcal{E}_{X}(\tau,x))dx^{2}+\tau^{2p(\tau,x)}(d\theta^{2}+\sin^{2}(\theta)d\varphi^{2}),\\
&\bigl|p(\tau,x)-p(0,x)\bigr|\lesssim\frac{|\log|(x)}{|\log|(\tau)},\ p(0,x)\approx x^{\frac{1}{2(s-1)}},\ |\partial_{x}p|(\tau,x)\lesssim x^{-1+\frac{1}{2(s-1)}},\\
&\phi(\tau,x)=p_{\phi}(x)\left(\log(\frac{x^{\frac{2s-1}{2(s-1)}}}{\tau})+\mathcal{E}_{\phi}(\tau,x)\right)+\varXi_{\mathcal{S}}(x),\\
&|p_{\phi}|(x)\approx x^{-\frac{1}{4(s-1)}},\ |\varXi_{\mathcal{S}}|(x)\lesssim x^{-\frac{1}{4(s-1)}},\\
&|\mathcal{E}_{T}|(\tau,x),\ |\mathcal{E}_{X}|(\tau,x),\ |\mathcal{E}_{\phi}|(\tau,x)\lesssim\frac{\tau^{2p(\tau,x)}}{x^{\frac{2s-1}{s-1}}}\left[1+\log^{2}(\frac{\tau^{2p(\tau,x)}}{x^{\frac{2s-1}{s-1}}})\right],\end{split}

(3.10)

where
p_{\phi}(x)\in\mathbb{C}
satisfies the usual Kasner relations

\begin{split}&p_{1}^{2}(\tau,x)+p_{2}^{2}(\tau,x)+p_{3}^{2}(\tau,x)+2|p_{\phi}|^{2}(x)=1,\\
&p_{1}(\tau,x)=1-2p(\tau,x),\ p_{2}(\tau,x)=p_{3}(\tau,x)=p(\tau,x),\text{ i.e., }p_{1}(\tau,x)+p_{2}(\tau,x)+p_{3}(\tau,x)=1.\end{split}

(3.11)

in the coordinate system
(\tau,x,\theta,\varphi)
, which relates to
(u,v,\theta,\varphi)
in the following way, with
(u,v)=(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},+\infty)
corresponding to
(\tau,x)=(0,0)
and
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}=\{\tau=0\}
and defining
x_{\mathcal{S}}(v):=\underset{u\rightarrow u_{\mathcal{S}}(v)}{\lim}x(u,v)
:

\begin{split}&\tau(u,v)=[r(u,v)]^{p^{-1}(u,v)},\\
&\bigl|x(u,v)-x_{\mathcal{S}}(v)\big|\lesssim\frac{r^{2}(u,v)}{r_{0}^{2}(v)}\left[1+\log^{2}(\frac{r^{2}(u,v)}{r_{0}^{2}(v)})\right],\ x_{\mathcal{S}}(v)\approx v^{2(1-s)}.\end{split}

(3.12)

In particular, the Kasner exponents and scalar field obey the following estimates in
(u,v)
coordinates:

\begin{split}&p(u,v)\approx v^{-1},\ |p_{\phi}|(v)\approx v^{\frac{1}{2}},\ \phi(u,v)=p_{\phi}(v)\log(\frac{r_{0}(v)}{r(u,v)})+\tilde{\varXi}_{\mathcal{S}}(v),\ |\tilde{\varXi}_{\mathcal{S}}|(v)\lesssim v^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
&|\mathcal{E}_{T}|(u,v),\ |\mathcal{E}_{X}|(u,v),\ |\mathcal{E}_{\phi}|(u,v)\lesssim\frac{r^{2}(u,v)}{r_{0}^{2}(v)}\left[1+\log^{2}(\frac{r^{2}(u,v)}{r_{0}^{2}(v)})\right],\end{split}

(3.13)

where
r_{0}(v):=r(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v)\approx v^{\frac{1}{2}-s}
as a consequence of (
3.2
).

iii.

If we assume that
[u_{0},u_{F})\times\{v_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{T}\cup\mathcal{A}
and
\underset{u\rightarrow u_{F}}{\lim}r(u,v_{0})=0
, then
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{F}
, so Statement
ii
holds.

Moreover, there exist (a large class of) initial data
\underline{C}_{in}\cup C_{out}=[u_{0},u_{F})\times[v_{0},+\infty)
such that the assumptions (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.8
) hold on
C_{out}
and
\underline{C}_{in}\subset\mathcal{T}
with
\underset{u\rightarrow u_{F}}{\lim}r(u,v_{0})=0
. So, for such initial data, the conclusion of Statement
ii
holds. These initial data are constructed as such: starting from the gauge choice

\begin{split}&A_{u}(\cdot,v_{0})=0,\\
&-r\partial_{u}r(\cdot,v_{0})\equiv-1\ \&\lim_{u\rightarrow u_{F}}r(u,v_{0})=0,\text{ or equivalently }u_{F}-u=\frac{r^{2}(u,v_{0})}{2},\end{split}

(3.14)

we then assume Kasner-like scalar field asymptotics, in that there exists a constant
|\psi_{0}|>1
such that

\begin{split}&|\phi|(u,v_{0})\lesssim\log(r^{-1})(u,v_{0}),\ \frac{|D_{u}\phi|}{|\partial_{u}r|}(u,v_{0})\lesssim r^{-1}(u,v_{0}),\\
&\liminf_{u\rightarrow u_{F}}\frac{r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v_{0})}{|\partial_{u}r|(u,v_{0})}\geq|\psi_{0}|.\end{split}

(3.15)

Under these assumptions, the following quantities are well-defined:

\begin{split}&I(\phi)=\sup_{u_{0}\leq u<u_{F}}\left(-r^{2}(u,v_{0})+[Q(u,v_{0})+q_{0}\int_{u_{0}}^{u}r^{2}\Im(\bar{\phi}D_{u}\phi)(u^{\prime},v_{0})du^{\prime}]^{2}\right),\\
&N_{0}(\phi)=\int_{u_{0}}^{u_{F}}r^{-2}(u,v_{0})\exp(-\mathcal{F}(u))|\partial_{u}r|(u,v_{0})du,\end{split}

(3.16)

where we have introduced the notation
\mathcal{F}(u)=\int_{u_{0}}^{u}\frac{r|D_{u}\phi|^{2}(u^{\prime},v_{0})}{|\partial_{u}r|(u^{\prime},v_{0})}du^{\prime}
. Then, we make the additional quantitative assumption that

2\rho(u_{0},v_{0})-r(u_{0},v_{0})>I[\phi]N_{0}(\phi),

(3.17)

which is in particular satisfied if
2\rho(u_{0},v_{0})-r(u_{0},v_{0})
is large with respect to
|Q|(u_{0},v_{0})
and
\phi(\cdot,v_{0})
. Moreover, (
3.17
) is also satisfied for a class of large scalar field initial data, specifically with Kasner asymptotics of the form

\phi(u,v_{0})=\Psi_{0}\log(r^{-1}(u_{0},v))+\tilde{\phi}(u,v_{0}),

(3.18)

where
\tilde{\phi}(u,v_{0})
is bounded and
|\Psi_{0}|
is sufficiently large.

Remark 3.1
.

Note that, instead of (
3.7
), Theorem 3.1 in
[
64
]
required the (stronger) assumption that

|\Im(\bar{\phi}D_{v}\phi)|(u_{0},v)\lesssim v^{-s-\delta}.

(3.19)

We proved in Proposition 6.5 in
[
64
]
that (
3.19
) implies (
3.7
), and subsequently we have only used (
3.7
) (and not (
3.19
)) in the proof of Theorem 3.1. Although (
3.19
) is a “cleaner” assumption to state, it turns out that (
3.7
) is simpler to achieve in practice and thus, in this manuscript we have decided to reformulate Theorem 3.1 as Theorem
3.1
with (
3.19
) instead of (
3.7
).

3.2
Applications of the local result to one-ended black hole spacetimes

Based on geometric extension principles, it is possible to isolate all the possibilities for spacetime boundary components for one-ended spherically symmetric solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
): this result was obtained in
[
41
]
, finding only a finite number of possibilities as seen in the following theorem.

Theorem 3.2

(Theorem 1.1 of
[
41
]
)
.

We consider the maximal development
(M=\mathcal{Q}^{+}\times_{r}\mathcal{S}^{2},g_{\mu\nu},\phi,F_{\mu\nu})
of smooth, spherically symmetric, containing no anti-trapped surface, one-ended asymptotically flat
initial data satisfying the Einstein–Maxwell–Klein–Gordon system, where
r:\mathcal{Q}^{+}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
is the area-radius function. Then the Penrose diagram of
\mathcal{Q}^{+}
is given by Figure
7
, with boundary
\Sigma\cup\Gamma
in the sense of manifold-with-boundary — where
\Sigma
is space-like, and
\Gamma
, the center of symmetry, is time-like with
r_{|\Gamma}=0
— and boundary
\mathcal{B}^{+}
induced by the manifold ambient
\mathbb{R}^{1+1}
:

\mathcal{B}^{+}=b_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\cup i^{+}\cup\mathcal{I}^{+}\cup i^{0},

where
i^{0}
is space-like infinity,
\mathcal{I}^{+}
is null infinity,
i^{+}
is time-like infinity and

1.

\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is a connected (possibly empty) half-open null ingoing segment emanating from
i^{+}
. The area-radius function
r
extends as a strictly positive function on
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, except maybe at its future endpoint.

2.

\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}
is a connected (possibly empty) half-open null ingoing segment emanating (but not including) from the end-point of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\cup i^{+}
.
r
extends continuously to zero on
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}
.

3.

b_{\Gamma}
is the center end-point i.e. the unique future limit point of
\Gamma
in
\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{+}}-\mathcal{Q}^{+}
.

4.

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}
is a connected (possibly empty) half-open null outgoing segment emanating from
b_{\Gamma}
.

r
extends continuously to zero on
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}
.

5.

\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
is a connected (possibly empty) half-open null outgoing segment emanating from the future end-point of
b_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}
.
r
extends as a strictly positive function on
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
, except maybe at its future endpoint.

6.

\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}
is a connected (possibly empty) half-open null outgoing segment emanating from the future end-point of
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
.
r
extends continuously to zero on
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}
.

7.

\mathcal{S}
is a connected (possibly empty) achronal curve that does not intersect null rays emanating from
b_{\Gamma}
or
i^{+}
.
r
extends continuously to zero on
\mathcal{S}
.

We also define the black hole region
\mathcal{BH}:=\mathcal{Q}^{+}\backslash J^{-}(\mathcal{I}^{+})
, and the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}=\overline{J^{-}(\mathcal{I}^{+})}\backslash J^{-}(\mathcal{I}^{+})\subset~\mathcal{Q}^{+}
.

We moreover record a useful definition from
[
41
,
62
]
:

Definition 3.1
.

We say
b_{\Gamma}
is a (central) first singularity if
\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
.

The main open problem in spherically symmetric black hole dynamics is then to determine which of these spacetime boundaries components
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
,
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}
,
b_{\Gamma}
,
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}
,
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
,
\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}
,
\mathcal{S}
, if any, is empty, and provide quantitative estimates on the spacetime metric and matter fields near the non-empty components.

A partial resolution of this open problem is provided in the main result of
[
62
]
reflecting the breakdown of weak null singularities, i.e., the fact that
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
cannot be the only non-empty boundary component in Figure
1
, providing
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is weakly singular (the weak singularity of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is, in turn, obtained in
[
59
,
60
]
).

Theorem 3.3

(Theorem 3.1 of
[
62
]
)
.

For a one-ended spacetime as in Theorem
3.2
, assume there exists an outgoing future cone emanating from
(u_{1},v_{1})\in\mathcal{T}
and reaching
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
on which
\phi
and
Q
obey the following upper bounds: for all
v\geq v_{1}
,

|\phi|(u_{1},v)+|Q|(u_{1},v)\leq C\cdot|\log(\rho)|,

(3.20)

for some
C>0
, and the Hawking mass
\rho
blows up

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\rho(u_{1},v)=+\infty.

(3.21)

Then the Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
breaks down, namely

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset.

We now give applications of the quantitative Theorem
3.1
to the global one-ended setting in the following Theorem
3.4
below, which also includes a new, simpler proof of Theorem
3.3
. The novel result of Theorem
3.4
is that
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
(unconditionally) and that locally-naked singularities on which
r>0
(defined as
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
) are the only obstruction to the presence of spacelike singularities described by the quantitative estimates of Theorem
3.1
. Theorem
3.4
below corresponds to the first statement of Theorem
IV
from the introduction.

Theorem 3.4
.

We consider a one-ended black hole interior in the sense of Theorem
3.2
and we assume that the estimates (
3.1
) are satisfied on some outgoing cone
C_{out}=\{u_{0}\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
with
(u_{0},v=+\infty)\in\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
. Then

A.

There is no collapsed light cone emanating from
i^{+}
, namely:
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
.

B.

The Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
breaks down, namely:
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset.

Now we make the initial data assumptions (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)-(
3.8
) on
C_{out}
and we moreover assume that there is no locally-naked singularity on which
r>0
, i.e.,
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}=\emptyset
. In particular, this assumption is satisfied if
\mathcal{A}
has an endpoint at
b_{\Gamma}
. Then,

C.

There is no locally-naked singularity on which
r=0
, namely:
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}^{1}=\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}^{2}=\emptyset
.

D.

There exists a non-empty singularity not emanating from
i^{+}
or
b_{\Gamma}
and on which
r
extends to
0
: namely
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
and for
\epsilon>0
small enough,
\mathcal{S}\cap(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
is a spacelike boundary in
\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{+}}
. Moreover, the quantitative estimates (
3.2
), (
3.4
) and the Kasner asymptotics (
3.9
)–(
3.13
) of Theorem
3.1
hold.

Finally, assume that there exists
u_{s}\in\mathbb{R}
such that for all
u_{0}\leq u_{s}
with
(u_{0},v=+\infty)\in\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, there exists
v_{0}(u_{0})
such that (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)-(
3.8
) hold on
C_{out}=\{u_{0}\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
.

E.

Then,
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is a weakly singular Cauchy horizon, in the sense of mass inflation, i.e., for all
u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
:

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\rho(u,v)=+\infty.

(3.22)

Remark 3.2
.

Notice that, comparing to Theorem
3.1
, Theorem
3.4
does not require a breakdown condition of the Cauchy horizon. However, Statement
C
and
D
require the absence of a locally-naked singularity
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
emanating from the center
\Gamma
, an assumption which only depends on the local structure of
\Gamma
and therefore cannot be excluded generically without a resolution of Weak Cosmic Censorship as we explain in the Section
1.3
.

Remark 3.3
.

It is useful to note that, by (
2.17
), (
3.1
) implies that
\underset{v\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}\rho(u_{0},v)=\infty
(Hawking mass blow-up) so Statement
B
of Theorem
3.4
is a slightly weaker result than Theorem
3.3
. However, the proof of Statement
B
is much simpler and shorter, as we will see in Section
5.2.2
.

Then, we turn to the construction of one-ended asymptotically examples to which Theorem
3.1
applies. Theorem
3.5
below corresponds to Theorem
II
from the introduction, and the main result of the manuscript.

Theorem 3.5
.

There exists a large class of spherically symmetric black hole solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
q_{0}\neq 0
with the following properties

•

Their induced initial data on
\Sigma
, a one-ended spacelike hypersurface, are asymptotically flat, free of trapped and anti-trapped spheres. Therefore, the MGHD of
\Sigma
is free of anti-trapped spheres.

•

Their event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
is future-complete and in the strict causal future of
\Sigma
. Null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
is also future-complete.

•

The Penrose diagram is given by Figure
1
, i.e.,
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\neq\emptyset
,
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}^{1}=\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}^{2}=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
.

•

\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is a weakly singular Cauchy horizon, in the sense of mass inflation, i.e., for all
u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
:

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\rho(u,v)=+\infty.

(3.23)

•

For
\epsilon>0
small enough,
\mathcal{S}\cap(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon]\times[v_{0}^{R},+\infty)
is a spacelike boundary in
\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{+}}
. Moreover, the quantitative estimates (
3.2
), (
3.4
) and the Kasner asymptotics (
3.9
)–(
3.13
) of Theorem
3.1
hold.

•

For
\epsilon>0
small enough,
\mathcal{S}\cap(v_{\Gamma},v_{\Gamma}+\epsilon)
is a spacelike boundary in
\overline{\mathcal{Q}^{+}}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{S}\cap(v_{\Gamma},v_{\Gamma}+\epsilon)
is spatially-homogeneous and described by an asymptotically Kasner metric of exponents
(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{3})
.

Remark 3.4
.

The construction obtained in Theorem
3.5
relies on three ingredients. The first is the novel spacelike-characteristic gluing strategy (see Theorem
3.9
) in order to glue any uncharged regular sphere to the Reissner–Nordström trapped region. The second are propagation estimates in the black hole exterior for small charge, using spherical symmetry to propagate from left to right (i.e., from the event horizon towards the asymptotically flat end) leading to Theorem
6.10
and Corollary
6.11
. The last ingredient is a refinement of the scattering estimates in the black hole interior first obtained by the author and Kehle in
[
40
]
(Theorem
6.22
), which allows to verify the assumptions of Theorem
3.1
under the Cauchy horizon from infinity
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
.

3.3
Applications of the local result to two-ended black hole spacetimes

While the main motivation to Theorem
3.1
is on its applications to the one-ended case modeling the physical process gravitational collapse, it turns out that Theorem
3.1
also applies to the two-ended case, which has been extensively studied as discussed in Section
7
.

A simplifying feature of the two-ended case is the absence of a center, i.e.
\Gamma=\emptyset
, so in particular locally-naked singularities
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}
cannot arise. On the other hand, for an open set of initial data, no breakdown of the Cauchy horizon occurs
[
25
]
and in this case the spacelike component of the boundary is empty, namely
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
, as in the rightmost diagram of Figure
3
. We summarize these results, together with the analogue of Theorem
3.2
below. It applies to the maximal development of future-admissible initial data
\Sigma
, namely under the assumption that
\Sigma=\Sigma_{A}\cup\Sigma_{B}
, where
\Sigma_{A}
satisfies
\partial_{u}r<0
, and
\Sigma_{B}
satisfies
\partial_{v}r<0
and
\Sigma_{A}
,
\Sigma_{B}
are connected (see
[
25
]
).

Theorem 3.6

(
[
24
,
25
,
41
]
)
.

We consider the maximal development
(M=\mathcal{Q}^{+}\times_{r}\mathcal{S}^{2},g_{\mu\nu},\phi,F_{\mu\nu})
of smooth, spherically symmetric, future-admissible two-ended asymptotically flat
initial data satisfying the Einstein–Maxwell–Klein–Gordon system, where
r:\mathcal{Q}^{+}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
is the area-radius function.

•

(
[
25
]
, Proposition 4.1, after
[
24
]
and
[
41
]
). Then, the Penrose diagram of
\mathcal{Q}^{+}
is given by Figure
11
, with boundary
\mathcal{B}^{+}
induced by the manifold ambient
\mathbb{R}^{1+1}
:
\mathcal{B}^{+}=\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\cup i^{+}\cup\mathcal{I}^{+}\cup i^{0}
.

•

(
[
25
]
). Assume that the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
settles quantitatively towards a sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström event
horizon and that the scalar field is small, then
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
; in other words,
\mathcal{B}^{+}=\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\cup i^{+}\cup\mathcal{I}^{+}\cup i^{0}
and the Penrose diagram is given by the rightmost diagram in Figure
3
.

In particular, the second statement of Theorem
3.6
shows that there is no analogue of Theorem
3.3
in the two-ended case, since the Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
can close-off a two-ended spacetime without breaking down.
We will prove, however, that there exists two-ended black holes such that
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
(recall Theorem
III
and see Theorem
3.8
below). The following result is an application of Theorem
3.1
to two-ended spacetimes such that
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
(in particular, by Theorem

3.7
provides a rather general result, conditional on certain estimates being satisfied, the two-ended analogue of Theorem
3.7
. Theorem
3.7
below corresponds to the second statement of Theorem
IV
from the introduction; note that any value
q_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
is allowed in Theorem
3.7
.
(
3.10
),(
3.11
),(
3.12
)

Figure 11:
General Penrose diagram of a two-ended future-admissible black hole, figure from
[

]
.

Theorem 3.7
.

We consider a two-ended black hole interior as in Theorem

and we assume that there exists
C_{out}^{R}=\{u_{0}^{R}\}\times[v_{0}^{R},+\infty)
with

) is satisfied on

and

A.

For

C.

Then,
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is a weakly singular Cauchy horizon, in the sense of mass inflation.

Finally, we also provide an unconditional construction that, however, only applies to the case

previously studied by Luk–Oh

. We note that this construction is very different from that of Theorem

and allows to construct a much larger class
6

6

Theorem 3.8
.

In the case
q_{0}=0
, there exists a large class
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
of two-ended asymptotically flat spherically symmetric black hole solutions of (

)–(

) with

and
