3.4
Spacelike gluing theorem

Theorem
3.5
relies on new gluing results, which we now present. Theorem
3.9
below corresponds to Theorem
1.3
from the introduction, and will be split into two sub-gluing statements in Section
6
: Theorem
6.4
and Theorem
6.9
, the underlying strategy of which consists in a spacelike-characteristic gluing approach.

Theorem 3.9
.

Let
D\in\mathcal{R}
an uncharged
C^{k}
sphere data of area-radius
R>0
,
0\leq q<1
,
\varsigma=\pm 1
, and

\frac{R}{1+\sqrt{1-q^{2}}}<M_{f}<\frac{R}{1-\sqrt{1-q^{2}}}

(3.24)

Assume also that
\frac{|q_{0}|M_{f}(1-q)}{q}
is sufficiently large. Then, there exists
R_{f}
satisfying the bound

M_{f}[1-\sqrt{1-q^{2}}]<R_{f}<M_{f}[1+\sqrt{1-q^{2}}],

such that
D
can be spatially glued to a trapped Reissner–Nordström sphere of Hawking mass
M_{f}
, charge
\varsigma qM_{f}
and area-radius
R_{f}
.

Note that, in the case
q=0
, Theorem
3.9
permits spatial gluing to Schwarzschild. We apply Theorem
3.9
below to construct scalar-field analogues to the celebrated Oppenheimer–Snyder solution (corresponding to Theorem
1.5
from the introduction) in providing spacetimes which are spatially-homogeneous near the center
\Gamma
, and Schwarzschild/Reissner–Nordström near timelike infinity
i^{+}
: this is the object of Theorem
6.1
and Theorem
6.7
, respectively. Note that in Theorem
3.9
below, we are allowed to prescribe the area-radius of the regular sphere
R>0
arbitrarily, which then constraints
M_{f}
to be comparable to
R
; however, in subsequent applications of Theorem
3.9
, such freedom will not be allowed, as we will require a sufficiently small
R
to evolve the equations towards the past (see already the proofs of Theorem
6.1
, Theorem
6.7
and Theorem
6.10
). A contrario, we will also see in the proof of Theorem
3.9
that
R_{f}
can be chosen more flexibly.

4
Previous intermediate results proven in our previous work
[
64
]

In this section, we summarize some of the results proven in our previous work
[
64
]
.

4.1
A priori estimates

We now place ourselves in the setting of Theorem
3.1
, Statement
i
and consider the Cauchy development of data on
C_{out}=\{u_{0}\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
and
\underline{C}_{in}=[u_{0},u_{F})\times\{v_{0}\}
.

Proposition 4.1
.

[Proposition 4.2 in
[
64
]
]. We assume that the estimates (
3.1
) on
C_{out}
are true. Then,
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
satisfies the estimate

u_{0}\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}\leq u_{F},

and there exists
v_{0}(D)>0
large enough so that
[u_{0},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)\subset\mathcal{T}
. Furthermore, for all
(u,v)\in[u_{0},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
, the following estimates hold

\displaystyle 0.9L_{-}\ v^{-2s}\leq-r\partial_{v}r(u,v)\leq 1.1L_{+}\ v^{-2s},

(4.1)

\displaystyle r^{2}_{CH}(u)+\frac{0.9L_{-}}{2s-1}\ v^{1-2s}\leq r^{2}(u,v)\leq r^{2}_{CH}(u)+\frac{1.1L_{+}}{2s-1}\ v^{1-2s},

(4.2)

\displaystyle\bigl|r|\partial_{u}r|(u,v)-r|\partial_{u}r|_{CH}(u)\bigr|\leq E(D)\ e^{1.9K_{-}v}.

(4.3)

The global breakdown of the Cauchy horizon in gravitational collapse (Statement
B
from Theorem
3.4
) also follows immediately from Proposition
4.1
. Note that Proposition
4.1
also proves Theorem
1.8
from the Section
1
.

From Proposition
4.1
, it is clear that the gauge (
v
) can be imposed, which we will do in the rest of the proof. We will also fix the gauge freedom so that
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}=0
(see Section
2
). Therefore, we have for all
u_{0}\leq u\leq 0
:

\begin{split}&(r\partial_{u}r)_{CH}(u)\equiv-1,\\
&r_{CH}^{2}(u)=2|u|.\end{split}

4.2
Sufficient condition for a local breakdown of the Cauchy horizon

The following proposition is important in the new proof of the breakdown of weak null singularities(Section
5.2.2
).

Proposition 4.2
.

[Proposition 4.3 in
[
64
]
]. Assume that the estimates (
3.1
) on
C_{out}
are satisfied. Moreover, assume that
[u_{0},u_{F})\times\{v_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{T}
and
\underset{u\rightarrow u_{F}}{\lim}r(u,v_{0})=0
. Then
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{F}
and for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{F}
, there exists
v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)<+\infty
such that

\lim_{v\rightarrow v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)}r(u,v)=0.

5
Conditional applications to one or two-ended spacetimes

In this section, we address the proof of the conditional results, Theorem
3.4
(one-ended case), Theorem
3.7
(two-ended case) and (the first part of) Theorem
1.7
(
C^{2}
-inextendibility result), and we relegate the proof of the (more difficult to prove) unconditional results Theorem
3.5
and Theorem
3.8
to Section
6
and Section
7
respectively.

Discussion of the assumptions

We emphasize that the main conditions in our conditional results are the assumptions (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) on an outgoing cone inside the black hole. In the two-ended case, these assumptions are
known
to hold for generic asymptotically flat spherically symmetric black hole solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) for
q_{0}=0
by the work of Luk–Oh
[
45
,
46
,
47
]
and
7
7

7

Indeed,
[
47
]
is a conditional result on the satisfaction of weak decay assumptions on the black hole exterior. These assumptions have been proved to hold, however, in the recent work of Gautam
[
33
]
, see the discussion below.
the recent work
[
33
]
(see also Lemma
7.1
in Section
7
). In particular, imposing the assumptions (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) is only motivated in the case
q_{0}\neq 0
(both in the one and two-ended cases) not covered by
[
33
,
45
,
46
,
47
]
, which is the only case allowing to study one-ended black holes as we explained in the introduction. To provide unconditional analogues of our results for generic Cauchy data would require to understand the dynamics in the exterior of the black hole for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the
q_{0}\neq 0
case, which is an open problem (see, however
[
61
,
32
]
for partial progress).

Strategy of the proof in the one-ended case

In the one-ended case, our conditional result – Theorem
3.4
– additionally requires the absence of a locally-naked singularity on which
r>0
, i.e.,
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}=\emptyset
, recalling the terminology of Theorem
3.2
. In view of Theorem
3.3
(which we reprove as part of Theorem
3.4
), assuming
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}=\emptyset
is sufficient to obtain a non-empty spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}
described by the estimates of Theorem
3.1
.

Strategy of the proof in the two-ended case

In the two-ended case, locally-naked singularities do not arise, since
\Gamma=\emptyset
(recalling the first part of Theorem
3.6
). However, we recall that the two-ended case presents the additional complication that a black hole spacetime may not contain a spacelike singularity, i.e.,
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
, as Theorem
3.6
demonstrates. Our conditional result, of course, only applies to black holes such that
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
. In Section
7
, we will show that the class of such black holes is non-empty as part of our unconditional results in the two-ended case (Theorem
3.8
), relying on the arguments leading to the proof of Theorem
1.8
.

5.1
A priori characterization of the spacetime boundary

In what follows, we prove a result of independent interest, which is that the Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
(which obeys estimates given by (
3.1
) that are essentially equivalent to mass inflation) cannot be followed by an ingoing light cone
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}
on which
r
extends to
0
. We also show that, if the Cauchy horizon breaks down, then
r
tends to
0
towards its endpoint. We also recall the notation
r_{CH}(u)=r_{|\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}(u)
.

Proposition 5.1
.

We assume that the estimates (
3.1
) on
C_{out}
are true, and that
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{F}
. Then

\begin{split}\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}r(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v)=\lim_{u\rightarrow u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},\ u<u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}}r_{CH}(u)=0.\end{split}

(5.1)

Moreover,
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\{u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{F},\underset{v\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}r(u,v)=0\}=\emptyset
and for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{F}
, there exists
v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)<+\infty
such that

\lim_{v\rightarrow v_{\mathcal{S}}(u)}r(u,v)=0.

Proof.

Note that by Proposition
4.1
,
(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v_{0})\in\mathcal{T}
. Since
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{F}
, and
\mathcal{T}
is open, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that
[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon]\times\{v_{0}\}\subset\mathcal{T}
and by the monotonicity of (
2.9
) we deduce that
([u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon]\times[v_{0},+\infty))\cap\mathcal{Q}^{+}\subset\mathcal{T}
(recalling from Section
2.1
that
\mathcal{Q}^{+}
is the notation used for the Penrose diagram).

Since
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{F}
by definition, then it means that for every
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u<u_{F}
, there exists
v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{+\infty\}
such that (as a straightforward consequence of the extension principle of
[
41
]
)

\lim_{v\rightarrow v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)}r(u,v)=0.

Let
V<\underset{u\in[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon]}{\inf}\ v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)
, a sufficiently large constant. We then integrate
-\partial_{u}\partial_{v}(r^{2})
on
\{u\in[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon],V\leq v\leq v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)\}
(adopting the convention that
v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)=+\infty
for
u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
) using (
2.4
) and obtain, exploiting the monotonicity of (
2.9
) and the fact that
\{u\in[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon],V\leq v\leq v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)\}\subset\mathcal{T}
(namely we use
\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\Omega^{2}(u,v)dv\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}}{|\partial_{v}r|}(u,v_{1})r(u,v_{1})
):

r^{2}(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon,V)-r^{2}(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon,V)+r^{2}_{CH}(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon)\leq\int_{u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon}^{u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon}\int_{V}^{v_{\mathcal{B}}(u)}\Omega^{2}(u,v)dvdu\lesssim\epsilon.

Taking
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
gives (using the continuity of
u\rightarrow r^{2}(u,V)
)

\lim_{u\rightarrow u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},\ u<u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}}r_{CH}(u)=0.

Then, using (
4.2
) we obtain

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}r(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v)=0.

Let us prove that
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\{u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{F},\underset{v\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}r(u,v)=0\}=(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},u_{\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}}]=\emptyset
by contradiction. Assuming that
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\{u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{F},\underset{v\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}r(u,v)=0\}\neq\emptyset
, we revisit the proof of Proposition
4.1
to show (
4.1
), (
4.2
), (
4.3
) are still valid on the larger rectangle
(u,v)\in[u_{0},u_{\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)\subset\mathcal{T}
, and we have
r_{|\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}(u)=0
for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}\leq u\leq u_{\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}}
, hence
[r\partial_{u}r]_{|\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}(u)=0
for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}\leq u\leq u_{\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}}
in a regular
u
-gauge. To choose the
u
-gauge for
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}}
, we first choose
v_{0}
sufficiently large so that

|r\partial_{u}r|(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2},

then choose the
u
-gauge to be for all
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u\leq u_{\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}}

r\partial_{u}r(u,v)=r\partial_{u}r(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}},v).

(5.2)

But, then we note that integrating (
4.3
) on the ingoing cone
\{v=v_{0}\}
under the gauge (
5.2
) gives

\frac{u-u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}}{2}\lesssim e^{1.9K_{-}v},

which, as
v\rightarrow+\infty
, gives
u-u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}=0
, which is obviously a contradiction.
∎

5.2
Conditional applications to one/two-ended black holes

In this section, we provide the proof of Theorem
3.4
(one-ended case) and of Theorem
3.7
(two-ended case). We will also address the first part of Theorem
1.7
.

5.2.1
Absence of an ingoing collapsed null cone
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}

Consider a one-ended spherically symmetric spacetime in the sense of Section
2.1
and under the assumptions of Theorem
3.4
. Then
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
follows directly from Proposition
5.1
, which proves Statement
A
of Theorem
3.4
. A similar result is obtained in the two-ended case (Statement
A
of Theorem
3.7
).

5.2.2
New proof of the breakdown of the Cauchy horizon

Statement
B
of Theorem
3.4
follows immediately from Proposition
4.1
. Indeed, consider a one-ended spherically symmetric spacetime in the sense of Section
2.1
and denote
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
the
u
-value of the future end-point of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, and
u_{\Gamma}
the
u
-value of
b_{\Gamma}
in the notations of Theorem
3.2
. Since we are assuming (
3.1
), by Proposition
4.1
, there exists
v_{0}
such that
\{u\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)\subset\mathcal{T}\subset\mathcal{Q}
for all
u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}
so we must have
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}<u_{\Gamma}
, and therefore
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
.

5.2.3
Non-emptiness of
\mathcal{S}
and Kasner estimates

We turn to the proof of Statements
C
–
D
of Theorem
3.4
, for which we assume
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}=\emptyset
. By Statement
A
of Theorem
3.4
,
\mathcal{S}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
. But
\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
by Statement
B
of Theorem
3.4
(breakdown of the Cauchy horizon), and there exists
v_{T}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}})\times[v_{T},+\infty)\subset\mathcal{T}\subset\mathcal{Q}
and
C>0
,
p>0
such that for all
(u,v)\in~[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-~\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}})\times~[v_{T},+\infty)

r(u,v)\geq Cv^{-p}>0.

(5.3)

Next, we show by contradiction that
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
. Assume not, i.e., that
\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
. Since
\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
, then it must mean that
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
. Then the endpoint of
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\Gamma}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\Gamma}
intersects the endpoint of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, which contradicts (
5.3
). Therefore,
\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
.

This means that the endpoint of
\mathcal{S}
intersects the endpoint of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
. Therefore, since
[u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}})\times[v_{T},+\infty)\subset\mathcal{T}
, one can construct a
\underline{C}_{in}=[u_{0},u_{F})\times\{v_{T}\}\subset\mathcal{T}
such that
r(u,v_{T})\rightarrow 0
as
u\rightarrow u_{F}
and apply Statement
iii
of Theorem
3.1
. This means that
\mathcal{S}\cap\{u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}+\epsilon\}
is spacelike for small enough
\epsilon>0
and described by the Kasner asymptotics of Theorem
3.1
. The proof of Statement
B
of Theorem
3.7
is obtained analogously.

5.2.4
Mass inflation

Finally, the proof of Statement
3.22
of Theorem
3.4
follows from the additional assumption that there exists
u_{s}\in\mathbb{R}
such that (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)-(
3.8
) hold on
\{u_{0}\}\times[v_{0}(u_{0}),+\infty)
for all
u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}<u_{0}\leq u_{s}
, which immediately leads to mass inflation in this interval, i.e., for all
u\leq u_{s}
:

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\rho(u,v)=+\infty.

Then, by the propagation of the Hawking mass blow-up to the future (see, e.g.,
[
62
]
, Lemma 4.9), this extends to the whole Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, i.e., for all
u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}<u\leq u_{\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}}

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi(u,v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\rho(u,v)=+\infty.

(5.4)

The proof of Statement
C
of Theorem
3.7
is obtained analogously. This concludes both the proofs of Theorem
3.4
and Theorem
3.7
.

5.2.5
Proof of the first part of Theorem
1.7

We conclude this section with the proof of the first part of Theorem
1.7
, i.e., the fact that the (one or two-ended) spacetimes of Theorem
3.5
and Theorem
3.8
are
C^{2}
-future-inextendible. Note indeed that the Kretschmann scalar
K=R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}R^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}
is infinite on
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}
, hence there can be no
C^{2}
-extension through
\mathcal{S}

[
41
,
45
]
. Furthermore, by mass inflation (
5.4
), there can be no
C^{2}
-extension either through
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
(see
[
60
]
), therefore the spacetime is
C^{2}
-future-inextendible. Thus, the first part of Theorem
1.7
is proved.

6
Unconditional constructions of one-ended spacetimes

In this section, we address the proof of the unconditional results in the one-ended case (Theorem
3.5
), building up on some of the soft arguments of Section
5
while providing a new strategy to construct examples of spacetimes satisfying (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) in Theorem
3.1
. This is, by far, the most involved section of the manuscript.

In the one-ended case, it is not possible to study (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in spherical symmetry with
q_{0}=0
(except if
F\not\equiv 0
), which is the only case where (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are known to hold: this well-known phenomenon is due to the presence a regular center
\Gamma
, which is incompatible with a non-trivial
F
in the
q_{0}=0
case (see
[
66
]
[Section 5] for a discussion). Therefore, we have to embrace charged scalar fields, which have more complicated dynamics than uncharged ones (see, e.g., the discussion in
[
66
]
). To circumvent this difficulty, we make use a gluing/scattering procedure in several steps to obtain an asymptotically flat solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
q_{0}\neq 0
, for which we show that (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are satisfied on an outgoing cone inside the black hole, which we summarize below. A more detailed outline of the construction is also available in Section
6.1
.

•

As a first step in Section
6.2
, we construct uncharged black hole solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
q_{0}\neq 0
, but
F\equiv 0
and a real-valued scalar field
\phi
(Theorem
6.1
). Near the center, the singularity is spacelike and modeled after a FLRW metric (Corollary
6.2
), while near
i^{+}
(including part of the trapped region) and
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, the metric is exactly that of Schwarzschild spacetime.
The initial data are regular, localized, posed on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, free of trapped surfaces and asymptotically flat, non-intersecting with the black hole region. The construction relies on a new spacelike-characteristic gluing strategy in the uncharged case (Theorem
6.4
). This step also allows to complete the proof of Theorem
1.5
in the uncharged case
(q=0)
.

•

In Section
6.3
, we want to “charge” the previously constructed spacetime (Theorem
6.7
) and replace the Schwarzschild event horizon by that of a Reissner–Nordström black hole. To do this, we glue the Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere
(U,v)=(\delta,v_{0})
to a (mildly) trapped Schwarzschild sphere
(U,v)=(\delta,v_{Q=0})
, invoking a new characteristic gluing theorem inspired by the Kehle–Unger strategy
[
39
]
(Theorem
6.9
).

As in the previous step, the singularity is FLRW spacelike near the center (Corollary
6.8
), while near
i^{+}
(including part of the trapped region and a Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
) and
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, the metric is exactly (sub-extremal) Reissner–Nordström. This leads to completing the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

•

We then need to generalize the charged black hole construction to an arbitrary, dynamical event horizon that converges to Reissner–Nordström but is not exactly Reissner–Nordström in Section
6.4
(Theorem
6.10
and Corollary
6.11
). Contrary to the previous exact Reissner–Nordström case, the construction of the asymptotically flat end is then non-trivial and involves quantitative estimates in the black hole exterior.

First, we choose our event horizon late-time behavior by picking
\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}
to be a given (smooth) function
\Phi_{H}(v)
(in Eddington–Finkelstein
v
from the gauge choice (
iii
)) such that for some
s>\frac{3}{2}

|\Phi_{H}|(v),\ |\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}|(v)\lesssim v^{-s}.

(6.1)

We then solve for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with initial data on the bicharacteristic hypersurface (in the gauge (
iv
))

\begin{split}&\underline{C}_{in}\cup[\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap[v_{0},+\infty)],\\
&\underline{C}_{in}=[0,\delta]\times\{v=v_{0}\},\\
&\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap[v_{0},+\infty)=\{U=0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty).\end{split}

(6.2)

where we want the ingoing data on
\underline{C}_{in}
to be exactly Reissner–Nordström. For compatibility at the intersection sphere
(U,v)=(0,v_{0})
, we want to cut
\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}
off: let
\chi_{0}
, a smooth, compactly supported function such that
\chi(x)=1
for
0\leq x\leq 1
, and
\chi(x)=0
for
x\geq 2
: we impose the initial data

\phi(U,v_{0})=0,

(6.3)

\phi(0,v)=\Phi_{H}(v)\left[1-\chi(v-v_{0})\right].

(6.4)

Assuming
\delta
small enough, Theorem
1.9
allows to solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the spacetime rectangle
(U,v)\in[0,\delta]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
and shows the presence of a non-empty Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
.

We can then glue the resulting spacetime region to that of Theorem
6.1
and obtain a spacetime “to the left of its event horizon” (Proposition
6.14
).

To complete the proof of Theorem
3.5
, we still need to construct the right-part of the Penrose diagram. To construct the asymptotically flat end, we use a “scattering” argument in the black hole exterior starting from event horizon outgoing data and regular ingoing data and propagating from left to right, taking advantage of spherical symmetry (Propositions
6.15
–
6.18
, Corollary
6.19
, Lemma
6.20
and Proposition
6.21
). This step is strongly inspired from the author’s previous work
[
61
,
65
]
and takes advantage of the smallness of the black hole final charge.

•

In Section
6.5
, we turn to the “middle-part of the Penrose diagram”; this spacetime region has already been constructed in the previous steps, but we seek precise information of the metric there (Theorem
6.22
and Corollary
6.23
). The goal is to show that the assumptions (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) of Theorem
3.1
are satisfied inside the black hole. The proof relies on estimates from
[
59
]
(for (
3.1
), (
3.3
)), and a refinement of the scattering estimates in
[
40
]
for (
3.6
)–(
3.8
).

•

In Section
6.6
, we complete the construction by applying Theorem
3.1
to obtain the coexistence of null and spacelike singularities in the interior of our one-ended black hole. In addition to the null boundary
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
(Cauchy horizon), we note that the only remaining boundary component is
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}
(in the terminology of Theorem
3.2
). While
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}
need not be entirely spacelike (it might have null segments), it has by construction two distinct spacelike subsets which are both tidally contractive:

–

An isotropic FLRW-like spacelike singularity near the center

\Gamma
, with
(\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{3})
Kasner exponents.

–

A spacelike singularity with positive Kasner exponents degenerating to
(1,0,0)
as
v\rightarrow+\infty
, as a direct application of Theorem
3.1
.

6.1
The detailed outline of the construction

Now we start the proof of Theorem
3.5
. We describe a first construction of a spherically symmetric spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi,Q)
solving (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) satisfying the following properties: for any arbitrarily large
k\geq 2
:

1.

(\mathcal{M},g,\phi,Q)
is the MGHD of one-ended asymptotically flat
C^{k}
spherically symmetric initial data on some spacelike
\Sigma\approx\mathbb{R}^{3}
, with no trapped or anti-trapped surfaces.
b_{\Gamma}=(u_{\Gamma},v_{\Gamma})
denotes the endpoint of
\Gamma\neq\emptyset
.

2.

There exists a
v_{Q=0}>v_{\Gamma}
such that
F\equiv 0
on
\mathcal{M}\cap\{v\leq v_{Q=0}\}
(Einstein-scalar-field solution).

3.

There exists
v_{\Gamma}<v_{H}<v_{Q=0}
such that
g
is spatially-homogeneous on
\mathcal{M}\cap\{v\leq v_{Q=0}\}
(FLRW metric). Moreover, denoting
\mathcal{B}
, the future boundary of
\mathcal{M}
,
\mathcal{B}\cap\{v_{\Gamma}\leq v\leq v_{H}\}
is a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}_{H}\subset\mathcal{S}\subset\{r=0\}
.

4.

The following estimates are satisfied on the event horizon
\{u=u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v\geq v_{0}\}
, in the gauge choices (
iii
), (
2.37
), for some
s>\frac{3}{2}
and for a constant
\omega_{res}\in\mathbb{R}-\{0\}
to be fixed later, depending on the black hole parameters and
\delta>0

\begin{split}&|\phi|(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v)\approx v^{-s},\ |\partial_{v}(\phi e^{iq_{0}\omega_{res}v})|(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v)\approx v^{-s-1},\ |\partial_{v}^{2}(\phi e^{iq_{0}\omega_{res}v})|(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v)\lesssim v^{-s-2},\\
&|\partial_{v}^{3}(\phi e^{iq_{0}\omega_{res}v})|(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v)\lesssim v^{-s-3},\ |\Im(\phi e^{iq_{0}\omega_{res}v})|(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v)\lesssim v^{-s-\delta}.\end{split}

(6.5)

5.

The event horizon is transversely
C^{k}
-regular, namely there exists
\epsilon>0
,
D(v_{0})>0
such that for all
u\in[u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}+\epsilon]
,
1\leq i\leq k
:

|\phi|(u,v_{0}),\ |D_{u}^{i}\phi|(u,v_{0})\lesssim D(v_{0}),

(6.6)

and moreover
\mathcal{H}^{+}
is located in the strict causal future of
\Sigma
.

From Theorem
1.9
, we know that
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\neq\emptyset
and that (
3.3
) is satisfied. (
3.1
) is partially satisfied (however, we have not yet proven a lower bound on
|\partial_{v}r|
or
|\partial_{u}r|
). We will come back to arranging that (
3.1
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are satisfied later using the scattering theory in the black hole interior.

Once (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are shown to hold, and after an application of Theorem
3.4
, the proof of Theorem
3.5
reduces to the construction of a spacetime
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi,Q)
solving (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
q_{0}\neq 0
, and satisfying
1
–
4
. To carry out this construction, we proceed as follows (see Figure
18
).

A.

Start with a spatially-homogeneous solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
(but
q_{0}\neq 0
), representing a FLRW metric with
\mathbb{R}^{3}
topology (Proposition
6.3
). Show that a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}_{H}\subset\{r=0\}
forms in finite time. Truncate this solution
\mathcal{M}
to only keep the region
\mathcal{M}\cap\{v\leq v_{H}\}
for some
v_{H}>v_{\Gamma}
then, denoting
\mathcal{B}
the terminal boundary of
\mathcal{M}
,
\mathcal{B}\cap\{v\leq v_{H}\}
is a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}_{H}
and
\mathcal{M}\cap\{v\leq v_{H}\}
contains a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}
with no trapped surface. This truncated solution contains a non-empty regular center
\Gamma
with
\Sigma_{0}\cap\Gamma\neq\emptyset
.

B.

We use the new uncharged spacelike-characteristic gluing result (Theorem
6.4
) to glue a FLRW sphere

\mathbb{S}_{H}=(u_{H},v_{H})
to a Schwarzschild trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}=(u_{S},v_{S})
. The first step is to glue
\mathbb{S}_{H}
to a sphere
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{A}}
belonging to the apparent horizon in a spacelike-fashion. We then glue characteristically
\mathbb{S}_{\mathcal{A}}=(u_{A},v_{A})
to a Schwarzschild trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}=(u_{S},v_{S})
. This construction can be completed to give an uncharged black hole with a Schwarzschild event horizon and a Schwarzschild asymptotically flat end (Theorem
6.1
/Corollary
6.2
). By Theorem
3.2
, the terminal boundary only consists of a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}\subset\{r=0\}
.

C.

Let
v_{RN}>v_{S}
. We will glue the Schwarzschild trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}=(u_{S},v_{S})
to a Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}=(u_{RN},v_{RN})
via a new characteristic gluing result (Theorem
6.9
) inspired from
[
39
]
. As in
[
39
]
, this step imposes restrictions on the Reissner–Nordström parameters (small charge). This construction can be completed to produce a charged black hole with sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström event horizon, a Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}
and a Reissner–Nordström asymptotically flat end (Theorem
6.7
/Corollary
6.8
).

D.

We trivially glue the Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}=(u_{RN},v_{0})
towards a Reissner–Nordström regular sphere
\mathbb{S}_{H}=(u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},v_{0})
in the ingoing, past direction. Then, we impose event horizon data as in (
6.4
) satisfying (
6.5
) on the outgoing cone
\mathcal{H}^{+}=\{u=u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
, which will turn out to be the black hole’s event horizon once the construction is completed.

E.

We pose regular ingoing data on
[u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}]\times\{v^{max}\}
smoothly connecting to (
6.3
) at the sphere
(U,v)=(0,v_{0})
, and we solve sideways (taking advantage of spherical symmetry) to obtain a solution in the spacetime rectangle
[u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-\epsilon,u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
comprising a portion of null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-\epsilon\leq u\leq u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},\ v=+\infty\}
. For this step, we use the smallness of
\epsilon
and of the black hole charge
e
.

F.

Impose compactly supported (or more generally decaying at a rate
|u|^{-q}
for
q\gg 1
as
u\rightarrow-\infty
) data on
\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap\{-\infty<u\leq u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-\epsilon\}
and combined with the outgoing data on
\{u=u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}-\epsilon\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
, and solve backwards up to the asymptotically flat end
i^{0}
. We have constructed the right part of the Penrose diagram.

G.

We invoke Theorem
1.9
to show the existence of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, a null Cauchy horizon in the black hole interior and estimates in a spacetime rectangle
[u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}},u_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}+\epsilon]\times[v_{s},+\infty)
, where
v_{s}
is a large constant.

H.

We use a refinement of the nonlinear scattering arguments based on
[
40
]
to prove that (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are satisfied inside the black hole for profiles satisfying (
6.5
) (a much more refined assumption than (
6.1
)).

I.

We note the presence of an ingoing trapped cone
\underline{C}_{in}\subset\{v=v_{s}\}
towards which
\{r=0\}
, which is part of the assumptions of Theorem
3.1
.

Carrying out these Steps
A
–
I
will conclude the proof as an application of Theorem
3.4
.

We note that Steps
B
–
G
provides a general result (Theorem
6.10
) allowing to glue any uncharged spacetime region (on the left) to an asymptotically flat charged black hole converging to Reissner–Nordström of small charge but with arbitrary event horizon late-time tails prescribed by (
6.4
) (modulo the mild decay assumption (
6.1
), which must be satisfied with
s>\frac{3}{2}
). It is precisely this freedom which allows to satisfy the assumptions of Theorem
3.1
, after we specify a well-chosen profile
\Phi_{H}(v)
from (
6.4
) such that (
6.5
) is satisfied.

6.2
Construction of uncharged one-ended black hole spacetimes

In this section, we consider real-valued solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
q_{0}\neq 0
, but
F\equiv 0
,
A\equiv 0
(Einstein-scalar-field model). We start with the following uncharged gluing result, allowing to glue any spherically symmetric spacetime to a Schwarzschild black hole interior.
We recall the definition of a first singularity from Definition
3.1
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
and
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
, a subset of the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a hypersurface
\Sigma_{L}
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres and such that
b_{\Gamma}
is a first singularity.

Then, there exists
C^{k}
solutions
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
with the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
is the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres.

•

The black hole region of
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
is non-empty with an event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and, moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma
, i.e., it is located in the strict causal future of
\Sigma
.

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{S}
and an outgoing null
C_{S}
intersecting at a trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}

such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{S})\cap J^{-}(C_{S})
is isometric to a Schwarzschild metric with some mass
M>0
. In particular,
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
is a Schwarzschild trapped sphere, and
\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{S})
is coincides with an affine complete portion of a Schwarzschild event horizon.

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
coincides with
\mathcal{M}_{L}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
. Moreover,
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
can be chosen to be in the complement of the causal past of
b_{\Gamma}
.

Remark 6.1
.

In Theorem
6.1
, as stated, we are not allowed to fix the final black hole mass
M>0
(although the proof reveals a finite permissible range). This is only due to the construction of an initial hypersurface
\Sigma
that does not intersect the event horizon. Should this condition be relaxed to merely obtaining an initial hypersurface
\Sigma
free of trapped or anti-trapped surfaces, one can then choose any mass above a minimal value determined by
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
, as the proof of Theorem
6.4
shows.

Next, we apply Theorem
6.1
to the case where
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
is a FLRW spacetime near the center
\Gamma
(Corollary
6.2
). This results in the construction of scalar-field analogues of the celebrated Oppenheimer–Snyder solution corresponding to Theorem
1.5
in the case
q=0
.

The uncharged gluing procedure in Theorem
6.1
, and its application to the construction of a class of spacetimes in Corollary
6.2
are depicted in Figure
12
.

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
.
There exists
C^{k}
solutions
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
with the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
is the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres.

•

The black hole region of
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
is non-empty with an event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and, moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma
, i.e., it is located in the strict causal future of
\Sigma
.

•

The MGHD terminal boundary of
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
is

\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\},

(6.7)

a spacelike singularity.

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{S}
and an outgoing null
C_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
intersecting at a trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}

such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{S})\cap J^{-}(C_{S})
is isometric to a Schwarzschild metric with some mass
M>0
. In particular,
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
is a Schwarzschild trapped sphere and
\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{S})
coincides with a future affine complete portion of a Schwarzschild event horizon.

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
in the complement of the causal past of
b_{\Gamma}
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
is spatially homogeneous. Moreover,
\mathcal{S}_{H}:=\mathcal{S}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
is spacelike and coincides with the singularity of a FLRW metric with
\mathbb{R}^{3}
topology.

6.2.1
FLRW spacetimes

In this section, we consider so-called collapsing FLRW metrics
g
on
[0,T_{S})\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
of the following form

g=-dt^{2}+a^{2}(t)\left(d\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\right).

(6.8)

Remark 6.2
.

Note that (
6.8
) is not asymptotically flat (since, precisely, it is spatially-homogeneous with non-trivial
a(t)
). However, it will later be truncated to obtain an asymptotically flat end, while retaining the region near the center
\Gamma
, notably a subset of its spacelike singularity
\{t=T_{S}\}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

We look for solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
, which translates into the following system of ODEs

\ddot{a}(t)+3(\dot{\phi})^{2}a(t)=0,

(6.9)

a^{3}(t)\dot{\phi}(t)=a_{0}^{3}\dot{\phi_{0}},

(6.10)

where
\dot{f}
denotes
\frac{df(t)}{dt}
and
f_{0}
denotes
f(t=0)
.

Viewing
(g,\phi)
as a spherically symmetric solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
), we can define its area-radius function
r(t,\rho)=a(t)\rho
and
\mathcal{A}
the apparent horizon,
\mathcal{R}
the regular region and
\mathcal{T}
the trapped region as in Section
2.1
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
(g,\phi)
be a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
on
[0,T_{S})\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
of the form (
6.8
) with initial data

\begin{split}a(t=0)=a_{0}>0,\ \dot{a}(t=0)<0,\ \dot{\phi_{0}}\neq 0.\end{split}

(6.11)

Then,
0<T_{S}<\infty
, and
\mathcal{S}=\{T=T_{S}\}
can be attached as a future spacetime boundary, a spacelike singularity at which
r=0
. Moreover,
(g,\phi)
takes the spherically symmetric form (
2.1
), where

\begin{split}&u(t,\rho)=A(t)-\rho,\ v(t,\rho)=A(t)+\rho,\ A(t)=\int_{0}^{t}a^{-1}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime},\\
&\partial_{u}r=\nu_{H}(t,\rho):=\frac{a(t)}{2}[\dot{a}(t)\rho-1],\ \partial_{v}r=\lambda_{H}(t,\rho):=\frac{a(t)}{2}[\dot{a}(t)\rho+1],\\
&\Omega^{2}=\Omega^{2}_{H}(t,\rho):=4a^{2}(t),\\
&r(t,\rho)=a(t)\rho.\end{split}

(6.12)

Finally, the following estimates hold as
t\rightarrow T_{S}
, defining
a_{S}=a_{0}\cdot\left[\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}|\dot{\phi}_{0}|\right]^{\frac{1}{3}}
,
\phi_{S}=\frac{-sign(\dot{\phi}_{0})\sqrt{2}}{3\sqrt{3}}
:

a(t)\sim a_{S}\cdot(T_{S}-t)^{\frac{1}{3}},\ \dot{\phi}(t)\sim\frac{-\phi_{S}}{T_{S}-t},\ \phi(t)\sim\phi_{S}\ln(T_{S}-t).

(6.13)

Denoting
\rho_{\mathcal{A}}(t)>0
such that
(\rho_{\mathcal{A}}(t),t)\in\mathcal{A}
, and
\rho_{v_{0}}(t)
such that
v(t,\rho_{v_{0}}(t))=v_{0}
also gives the following asymptotics as
t\rightarrow T_{S}
:

\begin{split}&\rho_{\mathcal{A}}(t)\sim\frac{3}{a_{S}}(T_{S}-t)^{\frac{2}{3}},\\
&\rho_{v_{0}}(t)-v_{0}\sim\frac{3}{2a_{S}}(T_{S}-t)^{\frac{2}{3}}.\end{split}.

(6.14)

Proof.

We introduce

A(t)=\int_{0}^{t}a^{-1}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}
and the null coordinates

u(t,\rho)=A(t)-\rho,\ v(t,\rho)=A(t)+\rho

noting that

du=a^{-1}(t)dt-d\rho,\ dv=a^{-1}(t)dt+d\rho,\ \partial_{u}=\frac{a(t)}{2}\partial_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho},\ \partial_{v}=\frac{a(t)}{2}\partial_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}.

and one can write the metric as

g=a^{2}(t)\left(-dudv+\frac{[v-u]^{2}}{4}d\sigma_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\right)

so that the null lapse is

\Omega^{2}=4a^{2}(t)>0

and the area radius is

r=\frac{a(t)[v-u]}{2}=a(t)\rho

Note that

\partial_{u}r=\frac{a(t)}{2}[\dot{a}(t)\rho-1]<0,\ \partial_{v}r=\frac{a(t)}{2}[\dot{a}(t)\rho+1]

So based on this, the apparent horizon
\mathcal{A}=\{\partial_{v}r=0\}
is necessarily spacelike, with

\mathcal{A}=\{\rho=-[\dot{a}(t)]^{-1}\}.

We also have

\Gamma=\{\rho=0\}\subset\mathcal{R}=\{\rho<-[\dot{a}(t)]^{-1}\},\ \mathcal{T}=\{\rho>-[\dot{a}(t)]^{-1}\}.

Recall indeed that
\dot{a}(0)<0
, and by (
6.9
),
\dot{a}(t)<0
for all
t\geq 0
. It is thus clear that there exists
0<T_{S}<\frac{a_{0}}{-\dot{a_{0}}}
such that

\lim_{t\rightarrow T_{S}}a(t)=0.

(6.15)

(
6.9
), (
6.10
) can be cast in the following equivalent formulation: defining
c(t)=\frac{a^{3}(t)}{a_{0}^{3}}
, we have

c(t)\ddot{c}(t)+9[\dot{\phi_{0}}]^{2}=\frac{2}{3}[\dot{c}(t)]^{2}

(6.16)

It is useful to subsequently introduce the variable
v(t)=\dot{c}(t)
and obtain the system

\begin{split}&\dot{c}(t)=v(t),\\
&c(t)\dot{v}(t)=\frac{2v^{2}}{3}-9[\dot{\phi}_{0}]^{2}.\end{split}

(6.17)

which can be solved by separation of variables as

\frac{dc}{c}=\frac{v}{\frac{2v^{2}}{3}-9[\dot{\phi}_{0}]^{2}}dv,

(6.18)

which can be solved as

c(t)=\frac{c_{0}}{\bigl|v^{2}_{0}-\frac{27}{2}[\dot{\phi}_{0}]^{2}\bigr|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\bigl|v^{2}(t)-\frac{27}{2}[\dot{\phi}_{0}]^{2}\bigr|^{\frac{3}{4}}

(6.19)

This means that

\lim_{t\rightarrow T_{S}}v(t)=-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}|\dot{\phi}_{0}|,

from which we deduce

\lim_{t\rightarrow T_{S}}\frac{c(t)}{T_{S}-t}=\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{\sqrt{2}}|\dot{\phi}_{0}|,

from which (
6.13
) and (
6.14
) follow.

∎

6.2.2
Uncharged spacelike gluing in spherical symmetry

Our objective is to construct a class of one-ended spherically symmetric solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
, which coincide in some spacetime regions with the FLRW solutions constructed in Proposition
6.3
. First, in this section, we turn to the spacelike gluing problem, which we will use a tool towards that goal.

For this, we must solve the spacelike constraints equations for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\equiv 0
in spherical symmetry. We start with a hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}=\{v+u=0\}
, which we parametrize by
\rho=v-u=2v=-2u
. We prescribe

\begin{split}&\partial_{v}r_{|\Sigma_{0}}(\rho)=\lambda(\rho),\\
&\partial_{u}r_{|\Sigma_{0}}(\rho)=\nu(\rho),\\
&\partial_{v}\phi_{|\Sigma_{0}}(\rho)=T(\rho),\\
&\partial_{u}\phi_{|\Sigma_{0}}(\rho)=X(\rho).\end{split}

(6.20)

Note that prescribing the function
\lambda(\rho)
corresponds to fixing the
v
-gauge, while prescribing the function
\nu(\rho)
corresponds to fixing the
u
-gauge, recall the introduction of gauge
i
. Of course, since
\rho=v-u
, the parametrization of
\Sigma_{0}
by
\rho
is thus fixed by the choice of
\lambda(\rho)
and
\nu(\rho)
. We then construct the area-radius
r(\rho)
, the Hawking mass
\varpi(\rho)
and the scalar field
\phi(\rho)
by solving the following constraint equations:

\partial_{\rho}r(\rho)=\lambda(\rho)-\nu(\rho),

(6.21)

\partial_{\rho}\phi(\rho)=T(\rho)-X(\rho),

(6.22)

\partial_{\rho}\varpi(\rho)=\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{2\varpi(\rho)}{r(\rho)})\left(r^{2}(\rho)\lambda^{-1}(\rho)T^{2}(\rho)+r^{2}(\rho)|\nu|^{-1}(\rho)X^{2}(\rho)\right).

(6.23)

We are now ready to state our main uncharged gluing result. We refer the reader to Section
2.6
for precise definition of what it means to glue two spheres “spatially”.

Theorem 6.4

(Uncharged spacelike gluing)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbb{S}_{R}
a regular uncharged
C^{k}
data sphere of area-radius
R
and Hawking mass
M>0
, with
R>2M
.

I.

Let
R_{A}>R
and
0<R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}<R_{A}
,
M_{S}>\frac{R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}}{2}
. Then,
\mathbb{S}_{R}
can be glued spatially within the regular region to an apparent horizon uncharged data sphere
\mathbb{S}_{A}
of area-radius
R_{A}
and Hawking mass
\frac{R_{A}}{2}
. In turn,
\mathbb{S}_{A}
can be glued characteristically to a Schwarzschild trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
of area-radius
R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
and Hawking mass
M_{S}
.

II.

Let
R_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
and
M_{S}
such that and
R_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}>2M_{S}>R
. Then,
\mathbb{S}_{R}
can be glued spatially within the regular region to a regular Schwarzschild sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
with area-radius
R_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
and Hawking mass
M_{S}
.
Moreover, denoting
\Sigma
the spacelike hypersurface connecting
\mathbb{S}_{R}
and
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
, one can arrange for the Cauchy development for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) of the induced initial data on
\Sigma
to contain an exact Schwarzschild ingoing cone emanating from
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
and with an endpoint in the trapped region.

Remark 6.3
.

As will be established in Proposition
6.5
below, it is
impossible
to spatially glue a regular sphere
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to a Schwarzschild apparent horizon sphere (since it would violate (
6.25
), which, in addition to being sufficient for gluing, is also necessary as the proof shows); in particular, one requires a non-zero scalar field for the final glued sphere to be marginally trapped. This is consistent with the uncharged characteristic gluing result of
[
39
]
, where a Minkowski sphere is (characteristically) glued to a Schwarzschild apparent horizon: in the case of
[
39
]
, the apparent horizon
\mathcal{A}
is indeed null exactly at the “gluing junction”, not spacelike: thus, any spacelike hypersurface reaching the Schwarzschild marginally trapped sphere immediately penetrates the trapped region.

Remark 6.4
.

We remark that the uncharged spacelike gluing of
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
is much easier to prove than the claim of Theorem
6.4
, Statement
I
. In particular, the true difficulty in Theorem
6.4
, Statement
II
is to prove the presence of an exact Schwarzschild ingoing cone terminating into the trapped region, which will be not true for a general spacelike gluing of
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}
. However, this more difficult statement is essential to our gluing strategy.

Figure 12:
The spacelike-characteristic gluing strategy in the proof of Theorem
6.4
, statement
I
.

In the proposition below, the
u
and
v
gauge will be determined by gauge
i
, as we will see in the proof.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathbb{S}_{1}\in\mathcal{R}
a regular
C^{k}
sphere data with area-radius
r_{1}
and mass
M_{1}>0
such that

r_{1}>2M_{1}.

(6.24)

Let
M_{2}>\frac{r_{1}}{2}
,
\mathcal{N}_{0}<0
and
(\varPhi,\varPhi_{u}^{1},...,\varPhi_{u}^{k},\varPhi_{v}^{1},...,\varPhi_{v}^{k})
a list of
(2k+1)
real numbers. Denoting
\theta_{2}=2M_{2}\varPhi_{v}^{1}
, assume that

1<\theta_{2}^{2}<1+\frac{1}{k-1}.

(6.25)

Then, there exists
\mathbb{S}_{2}\in\mathcal{A}
an apparent horizon
C^{k}
sphere data with area-radius
r_{2}=2M_{2}
, mass
M_{2}
such that, in
C^{k}
lapse-normalized gauge

\Omega^{2}_{|\mathbb{S}_{2}}=1,\ \partial_{u}^{i}\Omega^{2}_{|\mathbb{S}_{2}}=\partial_{v}^{i}\Omega^{2}_{|\mathbb{S}_{2}}=0\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq k.

(6.26)

the scalar-field on
\mathbb{S}_{2}
is given by
(\varPhi,\varPhi_{u}^{1},...,\varPhi_{u}^{k},\varPhi_{v}^{1},...,\varPhi_{v}^{k})
,
\partial_{u}r_{|\mathbb{S}_{2}}=\mathcal{N}_{0}
and
\mathbb{S}_{2}
can be spatially glued to
\mathbb{S}_{1}
in an uncharged way and within the regular region.

Proof.

Let
(\rho,\rho_{u}^{1},...,\rho_{u}^{k+1},\rho_{v}^{1},...,\rho_{v}^{k+1})
,
(\omega,\omega_{u}^{1},...,\omega_{u}^{k},\omega_{v}^{1},...,\omega_{v}^{k})
,
(\varphi,\varphi_{u}^{1},...,\varphi_{u}^{k},\varphi_{v}^{1},...,\varphi_{v}^{k})
corresponding to the sphere data
\mathbb{S}_{1}
. We know that
\rho>0
,
\omega>0
, and
\rho_{v}^{1}>0
by assumption. With no loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathbb{S}_{1}
is
C^{k}
lapse-normalized (gauge choice), i.e.,
(\omega,\omega_{u}^{1},...,\omega_{u}^{k},\omega_{v}^{1},...,\omega_{v}^{k})=(1,0,...0,0,...0)
. We also denote

\begin{split}&\rho:=r_{1}>0,\\
&2M_{1}:=r_{1}[1+\rho_{u}^{1}\rho_{v}^{1}]>0,\\
&\phi_{1}:=\varphi\in\mathbb{R}.\end{split}

(6.27)

Start with
\Sigma_{0}
a hypersurface parametrized by
\rho
, and isolate the “initial sphere”
\rho=\rho_{1}
and the “final sphere”
\rho=\rho_{2}
with
\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}
.
We will construct a triplet of functions
(r(\rho),\varpi(\rho),\phi(\rho))
for
\rho_{1}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{2}
,
which we will use to obtain a solution of the constraint equations induced by (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) on
\Sigma_{0}
in spherical symmetry.

We then fix the following initial conditions:

\begin{split}&\varpi(\rho_{1})=M_{1}>0\\
&r(\rho_{1})=r_{1}>2M_{1},\\
&\phi(\rho_{1})=\varphi\in\mathbb{R}.\end{split}

(6.28)

Let
\lambda(\rho)
,
\nu(\rho)
,
T(\rho)
,
X(\rho)
smooth, and freely prescribed but obeying the initial compatibility conditions (recall that
\partial_{v}
and
\partial_{u}
derivatives can be obtained from the
\partial_{\rho}
derivatives and the equations of Section
2.1
), i.e., for all
1\leq j\leq k

\begin{split}&\lambda(\rho_{1})=\rho_{v}^{1},\ \partial_{v}^{j}\lambda(\rho_{1})=\rho_{v}^{j+1},\\
&\nu(\rho_{1})=\rho_{u}^{1},\ \partial_{u}^{j}\nu(\rho_{1})=\rho_{u}^{j+1},\\
&\partial_{v}^{j-1}T(\rho_{1})=\varphi_{v}^{j},\\
&\partial_{u}^{j-1}X(\rho_{1})=\varphi_{u}^{j}.\end{split}

(6.29)

Moreover, we assume that
\lambda(\rho)
,
\nu(\rho)
satisfy the following conditions:

\displaystyle\lambda(\rho)>0\text{ for all }\rho_{1}\leq\rho<\rho_{2},

(6.30)

\displaystyle\lambda(\rho_{2})=0,

(6.31)

\displaystyle\nu(\rho)<0\text{ for all }\rho_{1}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{2},

(6.32)

\displaystyle\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho_{2}}[\lambda(\rho)-\nu(\rho)]d\rho=r_{1}-2M_{2}>0.

(6.33)

We will also denote their Taylor expansions at order
k
as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
as such

\begin{split}&\lambda(\rho)=\sum_{q=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{L}_{q}(\rho_{2}-\rho)^{q}+O((\rho_{2}-\rho)^{k}),\\
&-\nu(\rho)=\sum_{q=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{N}_{q}(\rho_{2}-\rho)^{q}+O((\rho_{2}-\rho)^{k}).\end{split}

(6.34)

We assume that

\mathcal{L}_{1}>0.

We also assume that
T(\rho)
,
X(\rho)
satisfy the integral condition

\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho_{2}}[T(\rho)-X(\rho)]d\rho=\Phi-\phi_{1}.

(6.35)

In the sequel, we will denote
\theta:=rT
and
\xi:=rX
.

Then, we finally construct
r(\rho)
,
\varpi(\rho)
and
\phi(\rho)
by solving the system of ODEs (
6.21
), (
6.22
), (
6.23
) with initial conditions (
6.28
).

By (
6.30
), (
6.32
),
r
is monotonically increasing, therefore
r(\rho)\geq r_{1}>0
. Therefore, by (
6.23
) (a linear equation in
\varpi
, for
r
,
\lambda
,
\nu
,
\theta
,
\xi
given) we have that
\varpi(\rho)
is well-defined for all
\rho_{1}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{2}
.

Now, let us show that
\varpi(\rho)>0
by showing a stronger result, i.e. that
\partial_{\rho}\varpi(\rho)>0
for all
\rho_{1}\leq\rho<\rho_{2}
. If we show this, then
\varpi(\rho)>M_{1}>0
so the mass will be positive.

Suppose, by contradiction, that there exists
\rho_{1}<\rho<\rho_{2}
such that
2\varpi(\rho)>r(\rho)
. Then, let
\rho^{*}=\inf\{\rho_{1}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{2},\ \frac{2\varpi(\rho^{*})}{r(\rho^{*})}\geq 1\}
. By (
6.28
),
\rho^{*}>\rho_{1}
and by continuity,
\frac{2\varpi(\rho^{*})}{r(\rho^{*})}=1
. Moreover, by the definition of
\rho^{*}
, for all
\rho_{1}<\rho<\rho^{*}
:

1-\frac{2\varpi(\rho)}{r(\rho)}>0.

(6.36)

By (
6.23
), we know that
\partial_{\rho}\varpi(\rho^{*})=0
.
Therefore, we have, as
\rho\rightarrow\rho^{*}

\begin{split}&\varpi(\rho)=\varpi(\rho^{*})+O([\rho-\rho^{*}]^{2}),\ r(\rho)=2\varpi(\rho^{*})+[\lambda(\rho^{*})-\nu(\rho^{*})][\rho-\rho^{*}]+O([\rho-\rho^{*}]^{2}),\\
&1-\frac{2\varpi(\rho)}{r(\rho)}=\frac{[\lambda(\rho^{*})-\nu(\rho^{*})][\rho-\rho^{*}]}{2\varpi(\rho^{*})}+O([\rho-\rho^{*}]^{2}).\end{split}

(6.37)

In particular, (
6.37
) shows that
1-\frac{2\varpi(\rho)}{r(\rho)}<0
for all
\rho<\rho^{*}
sufficiently close to
\rho^{*}
, a contradiction with (
6.36
).

Conclusion: for all
\rho_{1}\leq\rho<\rho_{2}
,
2\varpi(\rho)>r(\rho)
. Thus,
\varpi(\rho)>0
for all
\rho_{1}\leq\rho\leq\rho_{2}
.

Now, we can solve the linear equation (
6.23
) on
\varpi
using an integrating factor: we obtain for all
\rho_{1}\leq\rho<\rho_{2}
:

\frac{r(\rho)}{2}-\varpi(\rho)=G(\rho)\left[\frac{r_{1}}{2}-M_{1}+\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho}\frac{\partial_{\rho}r}{2G(\rho^{\prime})}d\rho^{\prime}\right],

(6.38)

where

G(\rho)=\exp(-\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho}[\frac{\theta^{2}}{r\lambda}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{r|\nu|}](\rho^{\prime})d\rho^{\prime})

(6.39)

Note that as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
,

\frac{\theta^{2}(\rho)}{r\lambda(\rho)}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{r|\nu|}\sim\frac{\theta_{2}^{2}}{2M_{2}\mathcal{L}_{1}}[\rho_{2}-\rho]^{-1},

(6.40)

hence
G(\rho_{2})=0
. In fact, there exists a constant
G_{0}>0
such that as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
,

G(\rho)\sim G_{0}[\rho_{2}-\rho]^{\frac{\theta_{2}^{2}}{2M_{2}\mathcal{L}_{1}}}.

(6.41)

We now assume that

\alpha:=\frac{\theta_{2}^{2}}{2M_{2}\mathcal{L}_{1}}>1,

(6.42)

therefore
\frac{\partial_{\rho}r}{G(\rho)}
is not integrable as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
, and it is easy to see that as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
:

G(\rho)\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho}\frac{\partial_{\rho}r}{2G(\rho^{\prime})}d\rho^{\prime}\sim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{2[\alpha-1]}[\rho_{2}-\rho],

(6.43)

which gives, as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
:

1-\frac{2\varpi(\rho)}{r(\rho)}\sim\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{2M_{2}[\alpha-1]}[\rho_{2}-\rho].

(6.44)

In fact, if we assume the following condition, which is stronger than (
6.42
):

\alpha:=\frac{\theta_{2}^{2}}{2M_{2}\mathcal{L}_{1}}>k,

(6.45)

we get a Taylor expansion of the following form:

G(\rho)\int_{\rho_{1}}^{\rho}\frac{\partial_{\rho}r}{2G(\rho^{\prime})}d\rho^{\prime}=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{2[\alpha-1]}[\rho_{2}-\rho]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{q=1}^{k-1}\frac{\mathcal{L}_{q}-\mathcal{N}_{q}}{\alpha-1-q}[\rho-\rho_{2}]^{q+1}+O\left([\rho-\rho_{2}]^{\alpha}\right).

(6.46)

Therefore by (
6.38
) and (
6.45
), we get as
\rho\rightarrow\rho_{2}
:

\begin{split}&\varpi(\rho)=M_{2}-\frac{\alpha\mathcal{N}_{0}}{2[\alpha-1]}[\rho_{2}-\rho]-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{q=1}^{k-1}[\frac{1}{\alpha-1-q}+\frac{1}{q}][\mathcal{L}_{q}-\mathcal{N}_{q}][\rho-\rho_{2}]^{q+1}+O\left([\rho-\rho_{2}]^{\alpha}\right),\\
&r(\rho)-2\varpi(\rho)=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{[\alpha-1]}[\rho_{2}-\rho]+\sum_{q=1}^{k-1}\frac{\mathcal{L}_{q}-\mathcal{N}_{q}}{\alpha-1-q}[\rho-\rho_{2}]^{q+1}+O\left([\rho-\rho_{2}]^{\alpha}\right).\end{split}

(6.47)

In particular,
\varpi(\rho)
is
C^{k}
on
[\rho_{1},\rho_{2}]
.

Let us define for all
\rho_{1}\leq\rho<\rho_{2}
:

\Omega^{2}(\rho):=\frac{-\lambda(\rho)\nu(\rho)}{1-\frac{2\varpi(\rho)}{r(\rho)}}>0.

(6.48)

By (
6.44
),
\Omega^{2}(\rho)
extends continuously to
\rho=\rho_{2}
and

\Omega^{2}(\rho_{2})=\theta_{2}^{2}-2M_{2}\mathcal{L}_{1}.

(6.49)

Note that, by (
6.47
),
\Omega^{2}(\rho)
is
C^{k}
on
[\rho_{1},\rho_{2}]
. Ultimately, we want to the final sphere
\mathbb{S}_{2}
to be lapse-normalized, in particular, we impose the gauge
\Omega^{2}(\rho_{2})=1
, which fixes the value of
\mathcal{L}_{1}>0
to be

2M_{2}\mathcal{L}_{1}=\theta_{2}^{2}-1.

(6.50)

and therefore requires, due to (
6.45
) that

1<\theta_{2}^{2}<1+\frac{1}{k-1}.

(6.51)

Recall that the prescription of
\lambda(\rho)
and
\nu(\rho)
fixed the
v
-gauge choice and
u
-gauge choice respectively (in particular at the final sphere
\mathbb{S}_{2}
). However, it is well-known that any
C^{k}
data sphere is
C^{k}
gauge-equivalent to a lapse-normalized sphere
[
39
]
; we denote
\Phi_{k}(\partial_{v}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{v}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{v}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{u}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}))
to be the image of
(\partial_{v}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{v}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{v}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{u}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}))
under the gauge transform that makes
\mathbb{S}_{2}

C^{k}
lapse-normalized.

Since we have already normalized
\Omega^{2}(\rho_{2})=1
,
\partial_{v}\phi(\rho_{2})
is unchanged (since
r(\rho_{2})
is gauge-invariant) and thus will still satisfy (
6.51
). Since the gauge-transform
\Phi_{k}
is a bijection, we can choose
(\partial_{v}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{v}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{v}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}))
and
(\partial_{u}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{u}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}))
so that, as prescribed by the statement of the proposition:

\Phi_{k}(\partial_{v}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{v}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{v}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}\phi(\rho_{2}),\partial_{u}^{2}\phi(\rho_{2}),...,\partial_{u}^{k}\phi(\rho_{2}))=(\phi_{v}^{1},...,\phi_{v}^{k},\phi_{u}^{1},...\phi_{u}^{k}).

(6.52)

Fixing
\partial_{u}r_{|\mathbb{S}_{2}}=\mathcal{N}_{0}
can also be arranged following a similar logic. This concludes the proof of the proposition.

∎

The next proposition offers a characteristic uncharged gluing result, allowing to glue an apparent horizon sphere
\mathbb{S}_{A}
to a Schwarzschild trapped surface. The key is that traversal scalar field derivatives
\mathbb{S}_{A}
are rigidly imposed by the dynamics, which makes the argument somewhat straightforward, but the result weaker. Ultimately we will combine Proposition
6.5
and Proposition
6.6
to prove Theorem
6.4
: the reason why a rather weak gluing result such as Proposition
6.6
is sufficient comes from the fact that Proposition
6.5
offers tremendous flexibility in prescribing the traversal scalar field derivatives at
\mathbb{S}_{A}
, which compensates for the rigidity of Proposition
6.6
.

In the sequel, the
v
-gauge will be fixed by gauge (
ii
).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon_{S}>0
and
r_{S}>0
. Let
\mathbb{S}_{A}\in\mathcal{A}
an apparent horizon lapse-normalized
C^{k}
sphere data with
(r,\varpi)=(2M_{A},M_{A})
with
2M_{A}>r_{S}
: in particular,
\rho_{v}^{1}=0
on
\mathbb{S}_{A}
. We fix
k
real-numbers
(\varphi^{1}_{v},...\varphi^{k}_{v})
such that in the lapse-normalized gauge,

(\partial_{v}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},...,\partial_{v}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}^{k})=(\varphi^{1}_{v},...\varphi^{k}_{v}).

(6.53)

There exists
k+1
real numbers
(\varphi,\varphi^{1}_{u},...\varphi^{k}_{u})
and
\mathcal{N}_{0}<0
such that, assuming

(\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},\partial_{u}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},...,\partial_{u}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}^{k},\partial_{v}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},...,\partial_{v}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}^{k})=(\varphi,\varphi^{1}_{u},...\varphi^{k}_{u},\varphi^{1}_{v},...\varphi^{k}_{v}),

(6.54)

\rho_{u}^{1}=\mathcal{N}_{0},

(6.55)

then,
\mathbb{S}_{A}
can be characteristically glued in the outgoing future direction to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
a trapped Schwarzschild sphere with area-radius
r_{S}
and Hawking mass
M_{S}>0
such that

1-\frac{2M_{S}}{r_{S}}=-\epsilon_{S}.

(6.56)

Moreover, the associated characteristic data can be chosen to have no
anti-trapped spheres, namely
\partial_{u}r<0
.

Proof.

We freely choose
\phi(v)
for all
v_{1}\leq v\leq v_{2}
, subject to the following conditions, for all
1\leq j\leq k
:

\begin{split}&\phi(v_{1})=\varphi,\\
&\partial_{v}^{j}\phi(v_{1})=\varphi_{v}^{j},\\
&\phi(v_{2})=0,\\
&\partial_{v}^{j}\phi(v_{2})=0.\end{split}

(6.57)

In the lapse normalization
\Omega^{2}(v)=1
, we solve
\lambda=\partial_{v}r
as such (using (
2.9
))

\begin{split}&r(v_{1})=2M_{A},\\
&\lambda(v_{1})=0,\end{split}

(6.58)

\partial_{v}\lambda(v)=-r(v)[\partial_{v}\phi]^{2}(v).

(6.59)

Then, we pose the following “final conditions” for
\xi
: for all
0\leq j\leq k-1

\partial_{u}^{j}\xi(v_{2})=0

(6.60)

and subjected to the following recursive system of ODEs in
v
obtained by formally differentiating in
u
the equation (
2.19
), i.e.,
\partial_{v}\xi=-\frac{\nu}{r}\theta
, and using the notation
\theta(v)=r(v)\partial_{v}\phi(v)
:

\partial_{v}\partial_{u}^{j}\xi=-\partial_{u}^{j}[\frac{\nu}{r}\theta]=-\partial_{u}^{j}[\frac{\nu}{r}]\theta+\sum_{q=1}^{j}\binom{j}{q}\partial_{u}^{j-q}[\frac{\nu}{r}]\partial_{u}^{q-1}[-\frac{\lambda}{r}\xi],

(6.61)

where we formally replace, in the above,
\partial_{u}r
by
\nu
,
\partial_{u}[r\lambda]
by
-1
,
\partial_{u}^{2}[r\lambda]
by
0
etc… consistently with (
2.4
) and the gauge choice
\Omega^{2}=1
. Note that the RHS of (
6.61
) contains at most
j-1
derivatives of
\xi
, and moreover, since
\theta
,
\lambda
,
\nu
and
r
are already fixed, it is a linear ODE in
\xi
which is straightforward to solve. In particular, the above procedure determines

\partial_{u}^{j}\xi(v_{1})

(6.62)

for all
0\leq j\leq k-1
.

Then, we solve for
\nu
and its
u
derivatives by initializing them at
v=v_{1}
: for all
0\leq j\leq k
:

\begin{split}&\partial_{u}^{j}\nu(v_{1})=\mathcal{N}_{j},\end{split}

(6.63)

where
\mathcal{N}_{0}<0
is imposed by assumption and
\mathcal{N}_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq k
are determined by the following lapse-normalization
u
-condition for all
1\leq j\leq k
, originating from (
2.8
):

\partial_{u}^{j}\nu(v_{1})=-\partial_{u}^{j-1}[r^{-1}\xi^{2}](v_{1}),

(6.64)

recalling that, by induction, the RHS is determined by the knowledge of (
6.62
).

Then, defining
n:=r\nu
and

(n(v_{1}),\partial_{u}n(v_{1}),...,\partial_{u}^{k}n(v_{1}))=(n_{0},n_{1},...,n_{k}),

(6.65)

where
n_{0}=2M_{A}\mathcal{N}_{0}
,
n_{1}=2M_{A}\partial_{u}\nu(v_{1})+\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2}
and similarly, the other
n_{j}
’s are uniquely determined from the
\mathcal{N}_{j}
’s in this fashion, using the recurrence relation induced by
\partial_{u}n=r\partial_{u}\nu+\nu^{2}
. Then we solve the following ODE for all
1\leq j\leq k
:

\partial_{v}n=-1,\ \partial_{v}\partial_{u}^{j}n=0,

(6.66)

which is trivially solved by

n(v)=n_{0}-[v-v_{1}],\ \partial_{u}^{j}n(v)=n_{j},

(6.67)

and again
\nu
and its
\partial_{u}
derivatives can be obtained straightforwardly using
\partial_{u}n=r\partial_{u}\nu+\nu^{2}
. We note that
\nu(v)<0
for all
v_{1}\leq v\leq v_{2}
(since
n_{0}<0
). This gives, in particular,

-\nu(v_{2})=\frac{2M_{A}|\mathcal{N}_{0}|+[v_{2}-v_{1}]}{r(v_{2})}.

(6.68)

Now, denote
F(v)=r(v)[\partial_{v}\phi]^{2}(v)
: by (
6.57
) and (
6.59
), the jets of order
k-1
of
F(v)
are fixed both at
v=v_{1}
and
v=v_{2}
, and
F(v)\geq 0
is non-identically zero.

Note that,

\begin{split}&-\lambda(v_{2})=\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}F(v)dv,\\
&\Delta r:=R_{A}-r(v_{2})=\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}[\int^{v}_{v_{1}}F(v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}]dv,\end{split}

(6.69)

Then, since
2M(v_{2})-r(v_{2})=r(v_{2})\nu(v_{2})\lambda(v_{2})
, we have, also using (
6.68
) and denoting
\Delta v=v_{2}-v_{1}
and
\Delta r=2M_{A}-r(v_{2})>0
:

M(v_{2})=M_{A}\left[1+|\mathcal{N}_{0}||\lambda|(v_{2})\right]+|\lambda|(v_{2})\Delta v-\Delta r

(6.70)

Clearly, we have, since
F\geq 0

\Delta r\leq[\Delta v]|\lambda|(v_{2}),

(6.71)

hence
M(v_{2})>M_{A}
. Let us fix
F
, respecting the above conditions. By choosing
\Delta v
sufficiently small, we can arrange that
r_{S}<r(v_{2})<2M_{A}
, since
0<r_{S}<2M_{A}
. By (
6.71
), one can arrange
F
in a way that

|\lambda|(v_{2})\Delta v-\Delta r<M_{S}-M_{A}.

Then,
|\lambda|(v_{2})
and
\Delta r
being fixed in this way, and invoking (
6.70
), we can choose
\mathcal{N}_{0}
such that

M(v_{2})=M_{S}.

(6.72)

The sphere
\{v=v_{2}\}
is
C^{k}
Schwarzschild with area-radius
r_{S}<r(v_{2})<2M_{A}
and Hawking mass
M_{S}
. This Schwarzschild trapped sphere can be continued to the future by an outgoing Schwarzschild cone with constant mass
M=M_{S}
up to the sphere
r=r_{S}
, which concludes the proof of proposition.

∎

We finally turn to the proof of Theorem
6.4
.

Proof.

For Theorem
6.4
, Statement
I
, let
R_{A}>R>2M
and
0<R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}<R_{A}
,
M_{S}>\frac{R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}}{2}
, and the regular sphere
\mathbb{S}_{R}\in\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{0}<0
and
(\Phi,\Phi_{u}^{1},...,\Phi_{u}^{k},\Phi_{v}^{1},...,\Phi_{v}^{k})
a list of
(2k+1)
real numbers such that
\Phi_{v}^{1}
satisfies

1<R_{A}^{2}[\Phi_{v}^{1}]^{2}<1+\frac{1}{k-1}.

(6.73)

By applying Proposition
6.5
with
(\mathbb{S}_{1},r_{1},M_{1})=(\mathbb{S}_{R},R,M)
,
M_{2}=\frac{R_{A}}{2}
, one can glue
\mathbb{S}_{R}
spatially to
\mathbb{S}_{A}\in\mathcal{A}
, an apparent horizon sphere with area-radius
R_{A}
, Hawking mass
\frac{R_{A}}{2}
and such that, in the lapse-normalized gauge
\Omega^{2}_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}=1
,
\partial_{v}^{j}\Omega^{2}_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}=\partial_{u}^{j}\Omega^{2}_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}=0
for all
0\leq j\leq k
, the following holds:

(\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},\partial_{u}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},...,\partial_{u}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}^{k},\partial_{v}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}},...,\partial_{v}\phi_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}^{k})=(\Phi,\Phi^{1}_{u},...\Phi^{k}_{u},\Phi^{1}_{v},...\Phi^{k}_{v}).

(6.74)

Then, we can apply Proposition
6.6
with
M_{A}=\frac{R_{A}}{2}
,
r_{S}=R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
,
\epsilon_{S}=\frac{2M_{S}}{r_{S}}-1>0
and
(\varphi_{v}^{1},...,\varphi_{v}^{k})=(\Phi_{v}^{1},...,\Phi_{v}^{k})
. We can then choose
\mathcal{N}_{0}<0
and
(\Phi,\Phi_{u}^{1},...,\Phi_{u}^{k})
so that the conclusion of Proposition
6.6
holds, and thus
\mathbb{S}_{A}
can be spatially glued to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
a Schwarzschild trapped sphere of area-radius
R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
and Hawking mass
M_{S}
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.4
, Statement
I
.

Now, we turn to the proof of Theorem
6.4
, Statement
II
. Let
R_{S}^{\mathcal{R}}>2M_{S}>R
. Let
\eta>0
to be a constant small enough so that

\begin{split}&2M_{S}[1+\eta]<R_{S}^{\mathcal{R}},\\
&R<2M_{S}[1-\eta].\end{split}

(6.75)

We will choose
R_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}=2M_{S}[1-\eta]
,
R_{A}=2M_{S}[1+\eta]
and apply Theorem
6.4
, Statement
I
with this choice. Let
\Sigma_{0}
, spacelike hypersurface connecting
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{A}
and
C_{0}
the outgoing light cone connecting
\mathbb{S}_{A}
to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
, see Figure
12
. Note that
C_{0}
is trapped, except at its past endpoint
\mathbb{S}_{A}
. Extend
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
to a past-directed ingoing light cone
\underline{C}_{S}
which is exactly Schwarzschild with Hawking mass
M_{S}
and past endpoint
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{reg}
of area-radius
R_{A}=2M_{S}(1+\eta)
.

Choosing
\eta>0
small shrinks the size of
\underline{C}_{S}
to
0
. Let us set bicharacteristic initial data for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) on the
C_{0}\cup\underline{C}_{S}
and solve backwards. For
\eta
small enough, invoking local-wellposedness, we obtain a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in a whole open spacetime rectangle
\mathcal{D}_{A}
bounded to the past by an ingoing cone
\underline{C}_{A}
emanating from
\mathbb{S}_{A}
in the past-direction and an outgoing cone
C_{-\eta}
emanating from
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{reg}
in the past-direction, see Figure
13
.

Figure 13:
The construction strategy employed both in the proof of Theorem
6.4
, Statement

and of Theorem
6.1
. The left part of the diagram involving
\mathcal{M}_{L}
and its terminal boundary
\mathcal{B}_{L}
is only relevant to Theorem
6.1
and can be ignored in Theorem
6.4
.
\Sigma
spacelike is constructed to be free of any trapped or anti-trapped spheres.

By Cauchy stability,
\partial_{u}r<0
on
\mathcal{D}_{A}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{A}
does not contain any anti-trapped sphere.

Moreover, by (
2.9
),
C_{-\eta}\subset\mathcal{R}
. Furthermore, by the monotonicity property
\partial_{u}\lambda<0
from (
2.4
) and the fact that
\mathbb{S}_{A}\in\mathcal{A}
, we also have
\underline{C}_{A}\subset\mathcal{R}
and thus
\mathcal{D}_{A}\subset\mathcal{R}
.

Now, we can solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) backwards with non-characteristic initial data on
\Sigma_{0}
: we obtain a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) on a small domain
\mathcal{D}_{0}
emanating from
\Sigma_{0}
in the past-direction. In particular, we denote
C_{0}^{past}\subset\mathcal{D}_{0}
an small outgoing cone emanating from
\mathbb{S}_{A}
in the past-direction (but not including its future endpoint
\mathbb{S}_{A}
). We note that, because of (
2.9
) again,
C_{0}^{past}\subset\mathcal{R}
, and again by Cauchy stability,
\partial_{u}r<0
on
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{0}
does not contain any anti-trapped sphere.

We can then pose bicharacteristic initial data on
C_{0}^{past}\cup\underline{C}_{A}
, and solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) backwards (see again Figure
13
): by local-wellposedness (taking
C_{0}^{past}
shorter if necessary), we obtain a solution in a small spacetime rectangle
\mathcal{D}_{past}
emanating from
C_{0}^{past}\cup\underline{C}_{A}
in the past-direction, and yet again by Cauchy stability,
\mathcal{D}_{past}
does not contain any anti-trapped sphere. Moreover, by (
2.9
) and the fact that
\underline{C}_{A}\subset\mathcal{R}
, we know that
\mathcal{D}_{past}-\mathbb{S}_{A}\subset\mathcal{R}
.

By Cauchy stability, it is then easy to construct a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{R}
connecting
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{reg}
, passing through
\mathcal{D}_{past}
,
\mathcal{D}_{A}
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
, as depicted in Figure
13
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.4
.

∎

6.2.3
Global uncharged spacetime constructions

We are now ready to turn to the proof of Theorem
6.1
and Corollary
6.2
as an application of Theorem
6.4
.

Proof.

We start with
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
, inducing initial data on
\Sigma_{L}
and fix
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to be a non-central sphere on
\Sigma_{L}
, with its induced
C^{k}
sphere data, in particular its area-radius
R
and Hawking mass
M
with
R>2M
. By assumption,
\mathbb{S}_{R}\subset\mathcal{R}
. Then by Theorem
6.4
, we can glue
\mathbb{S}_{R}
spatially to
\mathbb{S}_{A}
, which in turn can be glued characteristically to a trapped Schwarzschild sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
, see Figure
13
. Similarly to the proof of Theorem
6.4
, Statement
II
, we choose for some small
\eta>0
:

\begin{split}&R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}=[1+\eta]R,\\
&2M_{S}=[1+\eta]R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}=[1+\eta]^{2}R,\\
&R_{A}=[1+\eta]2M_{S}=[1+\eta]^{2}R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}=[1+\eta]^{3}R.\end{split}

(6.76)

Then, exactly as in the proof of Theorem
6.4
, we extend
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
to a past-directed ingoing light cone
\underline{C}_{S}
which is exactly Schwarzschild with Hawking mass
M_{S}
and past endpoint
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{reg}
of area-radius
R_{A}=2M_{S}(1+\eta)
. By taking
\eta
sufficiently small, invoking local-wellposedness and Cauchy stability, we construct in a similar fashion a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{R}
with no anti-trapped spheres and connecting
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{reg}
. We can then extend
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{reg}
to its past as the Schwarzschild metric exactly, and extend
\Sigma
into an asymptotic flat (in fact, exactly Schwarzschild) spacelike hypersurface, see Figure
14
.

In the present construction, however,
\mathcal{H}^{+}
intersects
\Sigma
, so to conclude the proof, we need to construct another spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}
strictly to the past of
\Sigma
such that
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma_{0}
. By local well-posedness, one can solve for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) towards the past up to a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{-\epsilon}
, and by Cauchy stability,
\Sigma_{-\epsilon}
is free of trapped or anti-trapped spheres and asymptotically flat. To conclude, we want to show that
\mathcal{H}^{+}
extends to the past up to the center
\Gamma
before intersecting
\Sigma_{-\epsilon}
, see Figure
14
.

Figure 14:
The intended construction of a spacelike hypersurface

. Both

The key is that, with no loss of generality, one can replace
\Sigma_{L}
by
\Sigma_{L}^{\prime}
, another spacelike hypersurface in
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
to the future of
\Sigma_{L}
, and arbitrarily close to the terminal boundary of
\mathcal{M}_{L}
. Then, since
b_{\Gamma}
is a first singularity by assumption, we can integrate (
2.4
) (see
[
41
]
) to show that

\lim_{p\rightarrow b_{\Gamma}}r(p)=0.

(6.77)

We then redo the proof with
\Sigma_{L}^{\prime}
instead of
\Sigma_{L}
and denote
\mathbb{S}_{R}^{\prime}
the regular uncharged sphere to be glued, with area-radius
R_{L}^{\prime}>0
. Let
U_{L}>0
denote the
U
-coordinate of
\mathbb{S}_{R}^{\prime}
, corresponding to the new choice
\Sigma_{L}^{\prime}
and by convention, we fix the event horizon at
\{U=0\}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{H}^{+}\subset\{U=0\}
. Recall that, by construction, we can always choose
U_{L}
to be arbitrarily small, i.e., we can always choose
\mathbb{S}_{R}^{\prime}
to be arbitrarily close to
\mathcal{H}^{+}
as measured in the ingoing direction. Moreover, by taking
\Sigma_{L}^{\prime}
sufficiently close to the terminal boundary of
\mathcal{M}_{L}
, one can always choose
R_{L}^{\prime}
to be arbitrarily small while ensuring that
\mathbb{S}_{R}^{\prime}
is in the strict future of
\underline{C}_{\Gamma}
(the ingoing light cone emanating from
b_{\Gamma}
towards its past), as a consequence of (
6.77
). Proceeding exactly as above, we construct
\Sigma^{\prime}
, a spacelike hypersurface with no anti-trapped or trapped spheres which intersects
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. Let
\Gamma\cap\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{B}_{reg}\subset\Sigma^{\prime}
, an open ball containing the sphere
\Sigma^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}
, but whose boundary, the sphere
\mathcal{S}_{reg}
, is chosen sufficiently close to
\Sigma^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{H}^{+}
. By the previous discussion, for any
\eta>0
, one can assume that

\sup_{\mathcal{B}_{reg}}r<\eta.

(6.78)

6.1
, constructing a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}
strictly to the past of the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.
\Sigma_{L}^{\prime}
(thus
\mathcal{S}_{reg}
and

) is chosen sufficiently close to
\mathcal{B}_{L}
.

To conclude, we can solve backwards for (

)–(

) with induced spacelike initial data on

: choosing
\eta
sufficiently small then ensures existence within the whole past domain of influence

To see this, one can extend

) towards the past, up to a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma^{\prime}_{-\epsilon}
. Then, one can see that the ingoing light cone emanating from

\Sigma^{\prime}_{-\epsilon}
, providing the area-radius of

of
\mathcal{B}_{reg}

starting from

into this region and coincides with

at large

.

To prove Corollary
6.2
, first consider the spatially-homogeneous spacetime of Proposition
6.3
and let
\underline{C}_{\Gamma}
the ingoing cone emanating from
b_{\Gamma}
–the endpoint of the center
b_{\Gamma}=(T=T_{S},\rho=0)
–in the past-direction. Note that, by (
6.14
), the apparent horizon
\mathcal{A}
is spacelike for
t
sufficiently close to
T_{S}
, therefore
\underline{C}_{\Gamma}\cap\{t\geq T_{S}-\epsilon\}\subset\mathcal{R}
for
\epsilon>0
small enough.

So, we can create a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{L}\subset\mathcal{R}\cap\{t\geq T_{S}-\epsilon\}
which intersects the center
\Gamma
and whose endpoint
\mathbb{S}_{L}
is strictly to the future of
\underline{C}_{\Gamma}
, since
\mathcal{R}
is open.

It is then easy to extend

into an asymptotically flat spacelike hypersurface by solving the following ODEs (

)–(

) as in Proposition

, imposing
\lambda(\rho)>0
,

,

,

smooth to match with
\mathbb{S}_{L}
and assuming
\theta(\rho)
,
\xi(\rho)
compactly supported. One then obtains the MGHD
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
of the induced initial data on
\Sigma_{L}
, free of anti-trapped or trapped spheres, and such that
b_{\Gamma}
is a first singularity, so one can then apply Theorem
6.1
to conclude.

Note that the statement regarding the terminal boundary of the MGHD consisting of
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}
, a
C^{1}
-spacelike singularity follows from Theorem
3.2
together with the monotonicity properties specific to the
F\equiv 0
case, as first established by Christodoulou in
[

]
. In more details, we note that, in addition to
\mathcal{S}
, the only other possible boundary components are

, a Cauchy horizon emanating from the center and
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, a Cauchy horizon emanating from
i^{+}
. In this construction, the metric is exactly Schwarzschild near
i^{+}
, thus
9
9

9

Note that, more generally, we always have
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
if
F\equiv 0
as proven in
[
11
]
.

\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
. Moreover, the fact that the spacetime is exactly FLRW to the past of a cone
C_{H}
shows the terminal boundary is an (isotropic) spacelike singularity in this region, and therefore
\mathcal{CH}_{\Gamma}=\emptyset
too. This concludes the proof of Corollary

and of Theorem

in the case
