∎

6.3
Charging of the uncharged spacetime via trapped spheres gluing

In this section, we start from the uncharged spacetime constructed in Theorem
6.1
and we “charge” it via characteristic gluing, with a strategy similar to that of
[
39
]
. The main difference is that, here, we must stay away from the extremal case, the main object of study in
[
39
]
, since we glue an uncharged trapped surface to a charged one. Therefore, the argument does not strictly follow from
[
39
]
, although the methods are similar.

As a result, we will be able to show a charged analogue of Theorem
6.1
and Corollary
6.2
, as seen below. The strategy builds upon the methods previously developed in the proofs of Theorem
6.1
and Corollary
6.2
. It is also in this section that we will prove Theorem
3.9
.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
and
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
, a subset of the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a hypersurface
\Sigma_{L}
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres and such that
b_{\Gamma}
is a first singularity.

Then, there exists
q_{L}\in(0,1)
such that for all
q\in(0,q_{L})
and
\varsigma=\pm 1
, there exist
C^{k}
solutions
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\neq 0
with the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres.

•

The black hole region of
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is non-empty with an event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and, moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma
, i.e., it is located in the strict causal future of
\Sigma
.

•

There exist
M_{f}>0
and an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{RN}
and an outgoing null
C_{RN}
intersecting at a trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}

such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{RN})\cap J^{-}(C_{RN})
is isometric to a metric with mass
M_{f}
and charge
\varsigma qM_{f}
. In particular,
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
is a Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere and
\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{RN})
is coincides with a future affine complete portion of a Reissner–Nordström event horizon.

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
coincides with
\mathcal{M}_{L}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
. Moreover,
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
can be chosen to be in the complement of the causal past of
b_{\Gamma}
.

Next, similarly to Corollary
6.2
, we provide an application of Theorem
6.7
in the case where
\mathcal{M}_{L}
is a FLRW spacetime, which will also immediately give the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Corollary 6.8
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
. Then, there exists
q_{L}\in(0,1)
such that for all
q\in(0,q_{L})
,
\varsigma=\pm 1
, there exist
C^{k}
solutions
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\neq 0
with the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres.

•

The black hole region of
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is non-empty with an event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and, moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma
, i.e., it is located in the strict causal future of
\Sigma
.

•

There exist
M_{f}
and an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{RN}
and an outgoing null
C_{RN}
intersecting at a trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}

such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{RN})\cap J^{-}(C_{RN})
is isometric to a metric with mass
M_{f}
and charge
\varsigma qM_{f}
. In particular,
\mathbb{S}_{RN}
is a Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere and
\mathcal{H}^{+}\cap J^{+}(\underline{C}_{RN})
is coincides with an affine complete portion of a Reissner–Nordström event horizon.

•

The MGHD terminal boundary of
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is

\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\cup\mathcal{S},

(6.79)

where
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\neq\emptyset
(the Cauchy horizon) is a null boundary emanating from
i^{+}
on which
r
extends to a non-zero function, which is constant near
i^{+}
(Reissner–Nordström Cauchy horizon near
i^{+}
), and
r
extends to
0
on
\mathcal{S}
, which is a curvature singularity.

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
in the complement of the causal past of
b_{\Gamma}
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
is spatially homogeneous and moreover
F\equiv 0
in
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
. Moreover,
\mathcal{S}_{H}:=\mathcal{S}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
is spacelike and coincides with the singularity of a FLRW metric with
\mathbb{R}^{3}
topology.

Figure 16:
The spacelike-characteristic gluing strategy used in the proof of Theorem
6.7
, combining Theorem
6.4
and the new charged characteristic result Theorem
6.9
.

6.3.1
Stationary black hole outgoing: the trapped sphere case

Inspired by the gluing techniques via a charged scalar field of
[
39
]
, we now prove that a Schwarzschild trapped (or event horizon) sphere can be gluing to a Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere. Recall that to glue a regular Schwarzschild sphere to a Reissner–Nordström event horizon sphere in
[
39
]
, the authors require a large mass
M_{f}
/or small charge ratio
q
condition of the form:

\frac{|q_{0}|M_{f}}{q}\gg 1,

(6.80)

where
(e,M_{f})
are the charges and mass of the Reissner–Nordström event horizon sphere and
q=\frac{|e|}{M_{f}}\in(0,1]
. Obviously, if we require the final Reissner–Nordström sphere to be trapped instead of marginally trapped, we now must restrict
q=\frac{|e|}{M_{f}}\in(0,1)
. Moreover, we replace (
6.80
) by the more demanding condition

\frac{|q_{0}|M_{f}[1-q]}{q}\gg 1,

(6.81)

which now penalizes Reissner–Nordström trapped spheres that are too close to extremality. The following theorem below provides a precise statement of this new gluing result based on an adaption of techniques of
[
39
]
.

In this section, the
v
-gauge will be fixed by gauge (
ii
).

Theorem 6.9
.

[Characteristic charged trapped surfaces gluing]. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
be a small, but fixed constant. Let
\mathbb{S}_{i}
be a trapped or marginally trapped Schwarzschild
C^{k}
data sphere of mass
M_{i}
and radius
R_{i}
, with
R_{i}\leq 2M_{i}
, and
\mathbb{S}_{f}
be a Reissner–Nordström
C^{k}
data sphere of mass
M_{f}
, charge
e
and radius
R_{f}
. We assume the sub-extremality condition
q=\frac{|e|}{M_{f}}\in(0,1)
, and that
\mathbb{S}_{f}
is a trapped Reissner–Nordström sphere, namely:
R_{f}\in(r_{-}(M_{f},e),r_{+}(M_{f},e))
, where
r_{\pm}(M_{f},e)=M_{f}\pm\sqrt{M_{f}^{2}-e^{2}}=M_{f}(1\pm\sqrt{1-q^{2}})
. We also make the following largeness assumption

\frac{|q_{0}|M_{f}[1-q]}{q}\geq\delta_{0}^{-4}.

(6.82)

Moreover, we choose
0<R_{f}<R_{i}
such that the trapped Reissner–Nordström sphere of mass
M_{f}
, charge
e
and radius
R_{f}
is away from the event horizon and Cauchy horizon, more precisely:

1-(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{f}}{M_{f}}+\delta_{0}\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{i}}{M_{f}}<1+(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}.

(6.83)

Then, the
C^{k}
Schwarzschild (event horizon or trapped) sphere
\mathbb{S}_{i}
can be characteristically glued to the Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{f}
. The associated characteristic data can be chosen to have no
anti-trapped spheres, namely
\partial_{u}r<0
.

Proof.

The proof is inspired Theorem B in
[
39
]
, with some technical modifications. Like in
[
39
]
, we will impose the following ansatz for the scalar field: for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}^{N}
, we define
\phi(v)
for
v\in[v_{i},v_{f}]
as

\phi_{\alpha}(v)=[\sum_{j=1}^{k+1}\alpha_{j}\chi_{j}(v)]e^{-i\frac{v}{\Delta v}},

(6.84)

where the
\chi_{j}(v)
’s are smooth compactly supported cut-offs with disjoint support, and
\chi_{j}(v)
has support in
[v_{j-1},v_{j}]
where
\{v_{i},v_{0},...,v_{k+1},v_{f}\}
is an equipartition of
[v_{i},v_{f}]
with
v_{0}>v_{i}
and
v_{k+1}<v_{f}
. Note that
\phi(v,\alpha)\equiv 0
for
v\in[v_{i},v_{0}]\cup[v_{k+1},v_{f}]
.
We will also define

q:=\frac{|e|}{M_{f}}\in(0,1),

(6.85)

\Delta v=v_{f}-v_{i}>0,

(6.86)

and we will take

\frac{|e|}{|q_{0}|R_{i}^{2}}\lesssim|\alpha|^{2}\lesssim\frac{|e|}{|q_{0}|R_{i}^{2}}.

(6.87)

We also initialize various quantities (note, however, that the value
\partial_{u}r
is fixed at
v=v_{f}
) as such

Q(v_{i},\alpha)=0,

r(v_{i},\alpha)=R_{i},\

\partial_{v}r(v_{i},\alpha)=1-\frac{2M_{i}}{R_{i}}:=-\epsilon_{i}\leq 0,

-\partial_{u}r(v_{f},\alpha)=\frac{1-\frac{2M_{f}}{r(v_{f},\alpha)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(v_{f},\alpha)}}{\partial_{v}r(v_{f},\alpha)},

We will moreover, as in
[
39
]
, set the gauge to be
\Omega^{2}=1
,
A_{v}\equiv 0
, and solve the following set of ODEs with the above initial conditions

\begin{split}&\partial_{v}^{2}r=-r|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2},\\
&\partial_{v}Q=q_{0}r^{2}\Im(\bar{\phi}\partial_{v}\phi),\\
&\partial_{v}(-r\partial_{u}r)=1-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}.\end{split}

(6.88)

First, by monotonicity and (
6.83
), we have

r(v,\alpha)\leq R_{i}\leq 2M_{f}.

(6.89)

Then, note that differentiating (
6.84
) gives

\partial_{v}\phi\approx\frac{\alpha}{\Delta v},

(6.90)

and integrating (
6.88
) thus gives

|\lambda|(v)\lesssim\epsilon_{i}+\frac{R_{i}}{\Delta v}\alpha^{2}\lesssim\epsilon_{i}+\frac{qM_{f}}{|q_{0}|R_{i}\Delta v},

(6.91)

and thus, integrating in
v
again gives

\underset{v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{f}}{\sup}|r(v,\alpha)-R_{i}|\lesssim\epsilon_{i}\Delta v+\frac{qM_{f}}{q_{0}R_{i}}\lesssim\frac{qM_{f}}{q_{0}R_{i}}\lesssim R_{i}[\delta_{0}^{4}\frac{M_{f}^{2}[1-q]}{R_{i}^{2}}]

(6.92)

where the last two inequalities follows from (
6.82
) and choosing
\Delta v
small enough. Since we have chosen
R_{i}>R_{f}
satisfying (
6.83
), we know that

R_{i}>\frac{\delta_{0}M_{f}}{4},

(6.93)

hence by (
6.92
), we get

\underset{v_{i}\leq v\leq v_{f}}{\sup}|r(v,\alpha)-R_{i}|\lesssim\delta_{0}^{2}R_{i}[1-q]\lesssim\delta_{0}^{2}R_{i},

(6.94)

thus
r(v,\alpha)
is always comparable to
R_{i}
upon taking
\delta_{0}
sufficiently small. Furthermore, we can also arrange that

\frac{r(v,\alpha)}{M_{f}}\in(\frac{R_{f}}{M_{f}}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}\sqrt{1-q^{2}},1+(1-\frac{\delta_{0}}{2})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}).

(6.95)

Note that this already guarantees that
r(v_{f},\alpha)>R_{f}
. Then, integrating (
6.88
) gives

|Q|(v_{f},\alpha)\approx q_{0}R_{i}^{2}\alpha^{2}\approx|e|,

(6.96)

thus, as in
[
39
]
, we can choose
\alpha
so that

Q(v_{f},\alpha)=e,

(6.97)

exactly. Now, note that
r\partial_{u}r
at
v=v_{f}
is

-r\partial_{u}r(v_{f},\alpha)=r(v_{f},\alpha)\frac{1-\frac{2M_{f}}{r(v_{f},\alpha)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(v_{f},\alpha)}}{\partial_{v}r(v_{f},\alpha)}=M_{f}^{2}\frac{[1+\sqrt{1-q^{2}}-\frac{r(v_{f},\alpha)}{M_{f}}][\frac{r(v_{f},\alpha)}{M_{f}}-(1-\sqrt{1-q^{2}})]}{-r\partial_{v}r(v_{f},\alpha)}

(6.98)

As a consequence of (
6.95
), we have

-r\partial_{u}r(v_{f},\alpha)\gtrsim\frac{\delta_{0}^{2}M_{f}^{2}[1-q]}{R_{i}[-\partial_{v}r]}

(6.99)

Thus, combining (
6.98
) with (
6.91
) gives, by (
6.82
) and choosing
\Delta v
small:

-r\partial_{u}r(v_{f},\alpha)\gtrsim\Delta v\frac{|q_{0}|\delta_{0}^{2}M_{f}[1-q]}{q}\gtrsim\delta_{0}^{-2}\Delta v.

(6.100)

Finally, note that for all
v\in[v_{i},v_{f}]
,

-r\partial_{u}r(v,\alpha)=-r\partial_{u}r(v_{f},\alpha)-\int_{v}^{v_{f}}[1-\frac{Q^{2}(v^{\prime},\alpha)}{r^{2}(v^{\prime},\alpha)}]dv^{\prime}\geq-r\partial_{u}r(v_{f},\alpha)-(v_{f}-v)\gtrsim\delta_{0}^{-2}\Delta v>0.

(6.101)

To conclude and obtain zero ingoing scalar field derivatives at
v=v_{f}
, we use the argument of
[
39
]
: for each
A>0
,
F_{A}:\alpha\in\{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1},\ |\alpha|=A\}\rightarrow(\partial_{u}\phi(v_{f},\alpha),...,\partial_{u}^{k}\phi(v_{f},\alpha))
is continuous and odd, so by the Borsuk–Ulam theorem, there exists
\alpha^{*}
such that
|\alpha^{*}|=A
and
(\partial_{u}\phi(v_{f},\alpha^{*}),...,\partial_{u}^{k}\phi(v_{f},\alpha^{*}))=(0,...,0)
.

Thus, we have successfully glued the Schwarzschild event horizon
C^{k}
data sphere
\mathbb{S}_{i}
to the Reissner–Nordström trapped
C^{k}
data sphere of radius
r(v_{f},\alpha)
, mass
M_{f}
and charge
e
with no anti-trapped surface, i.e.,
\partial_{u}r<0
. Here,
r(v_{f},\alpha)>R_{f}
so to conclude the proof, one trivially glues the above Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere to
\mathbb{S}_{f}
by a Reissner–Nordström outgoing cone composed of trapped spheres.

∎

6.3.2
Global charged spacetime constructions

We are now ready to prove Theorem
3.9
, Theorem
6.7
and Corollary
6.8
.

Proof.

We will prove Theorem
3.9
and Theorem
6.7
together. Starting with Theorem
3.9
, we set
R>0
,
q\in[0,1)
,
M_{f}>0
satisfying (
3.24
) and
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
small such that (
6.82
) is satisfied.

Similarly to the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we choose for some small
\eta>0
:

\begin{split}&R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}=[1+\eta]R,\\
&2M_{S}=[1+\eta]R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}=[1+\eta]^{2}R,\\
&R_{A}=[1+\eta]2M_{S}=[1+\eta]^{2}R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}=[1+\eta]^{3}R,\end{split}

(6.102)

then, we apply Theorem
6.4
with these values, obtaining the spacelike gluing of
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to the apparent horizon sphere
\mathbb{S}_{A}
on some hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}
and then the characteristic gluing of
\mathbb{S}_{A}
to the trapped Schwarzschild sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
on some null cone
C_{0}
, in the notations of Theorem
6.4
and Figure
12
. Note that, by (
3.24
), we have

1-\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}}{M_{f}[1+\eta]}<1+\sqrt{1-q^{2}}.

(6.103)

By choosing
\eta=O(\delta_{0}\sqrt{1-q^{2}})
, one can then arrange (while keeping
\eta
small) that

1-(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}}{M_{f}}.

(6.104)

If
\frac{R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}}{M_{f}}<1+(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}
, we choose
R_{i}=R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}
. If not, we can find
R_{S}^{{}^{\prime},\mathcal{\tau}}<R_{S}^{\mathcal{\tau}}
such that

1-(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{S}^{{}^{\prime},\mathcal{\tau}}}{M_{f}}<1+(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}},

(6.105)

and glue trivially
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
to the trapped Schwarzschild sphere
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{{}^{\prime},\mathcal{T}}
of mass
M_{S}
and area-radius
R_{S}^{{}^{\prime},\mathcal{\tau}}
; after which we choose
R_{i}=R_{S}^{{}^{\prime},\mathcal{\tau}}
. Then, we set
R_{f}>0
such that

1-(1-\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{f}}{M_{f}}+\delta_{0}\sqrt{1-q^{2}}<\frac{R_{i}}{M_{f}}.

(6.106)

Then, we apply Theorem
6.9
with
M_{i}=M_{S}
, and
R_{i}
,
R_{f}
chosen as such; note that (
6.83
) is then satisfied. Thus, we glue characteristically
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{\mathcal{T}}
(or
\mathbb{S}_{S}^{{}^{\prime},\mathcal{T}}
) to the trapped Reissner–Nordström sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
of radius
R_{f}
, Hawking mass
M_{f}
and charge
e:=\pm qM_{f}
. We then extend the outgoing cone
C_{0}
to the future up to
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
. Note that, invoking local-existence and solving (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) forward from induced spacelike-characteristic initial data on
\Sigma_{0}\cup C_{0}
gives a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma^{\prime}
connecting
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
, which is free of anti-trapped surfaces by Cauchy stability; this concludes the proof of Theorem
3.9
.

Now, we continue with the proof of Theorem
6.7
. This time, we arrange (
6.82
) is satisfied by taking
q<q_{L}
sufficiently small and use the same strategy as in the above proof. We can then trivially glue
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
along a Reissner–Nordström ingoing cone
\underline{C}_{RN}
towards its past, up to a regular sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{R}}
of area-radius

R_{RN}^{\mathcal{R}}:=M_{f}\left[1+(1+\delta_{0})\sqrt{1-q^{2}}\right],

Hawking mass
M_{f}
and charge
e:=\pm qM_{f}
. Note that the size of
\underline{C}_{RN}
shrinks to
0
as
\delta_{0}\rightarrow 0
. Then, choosing
\delta_{0}
small enough if necessary, we can employ exactly the same strategy as in the proof of Theorem
6.4
, Statement
II
(Figure
13
, with an additional trapped Reissner–Nordström sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
on
C_{0}
): we can solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) backwards with bicharacteristic initial data on
C_{0}\cup\underline{C}_{RN}
, where
C_{0}
is the outgoing cone connecting
\mathbb{S}_{A}
to
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
. By local-wellposedness, we obtain a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) on a spacetime rectangle
\mathcal{D}_{A}
bounded to the future by
C_{0}\cup\underline{C}_{RN}
and to the past by
\underline{C}_{A}\cup C_{-\delta_{0}}
. By Cauchy stability, for
\delta_{0}
small enough,
\partial_{u}r<0
on
\mathcal{D}_{A}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{A}
does not contain any anti-trapped spheres. We also note that, in the lapse-normalized gauge imposed on
\mathbb{S}_{A}
, we have

\partial_{u}(r\lambda)_{|\mathbb{S}_{A}}=-1,

thus, denoting
\mathbb{S}_{A}=(u_{A},v_{A})
in the above gauge choice, we have for all
u<u_{A}
sufficiently close to
u_{A}
, since
\lambda(u_{A},v_{A})=0
, by definition

r\lambda(u,v_{A})\leq-\frac{1}{2}[u_{A}-u],

(6.107)

therefore, assuming
\delta_{0}
is sufficiently small, we still have
\underline{C}_{A}-\mathbb{S}_{A}\subset\mathcal{R}
, and of course,
C_{-\delta_{0}}\subset\mathcal{R}
. Proceeding exactly as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
,
we construct a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma\subset\mathcal{R}
with no anti-trapped or trapped spheres and connecting
\mathbb{S}_{R}
to
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{R}}
. We can then extend
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{R}}
to its past as the Reissner–Nordström metric exactly, and thereby extend
\Sigma
into an asymptotically flat (in fact, exactly Reissner–Nordström) spacelike hypersurface, analogously to Figure
14
.

Finally, we proceed as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
to construct another spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}
strictly to the past of
\Sigma
with no anti-trapped or trapped spheres and such that
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma_{0}
, analogously to the situation depicted on Figure
15
. Note that this step requires to take
R
small, and thus
M_{f}
is small as well. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.7
.

Once Theorem
6.7
is proved, the proof of Corollary
6.8
follows completely analogously from that of Corollary
6.2
. The proof of Theorem
1.5
is also obtained as an immediate consequence of that of Corollary
6.2
.
∎

6.4
Construction of the asymptotically flat end for dynamical horizons

The previous gluing approaches, materialized by Theorem
6.1
and Theorem
6.7
, allowed gluing of any regular (uncharged) sphere to an exact Schwarzschild or sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström trapped sphere, which was then extended as a larger Schwarzschild/Reissner–Nordström region (namely, an (electro)-vacuum solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
)), including both an open neighborhood of timelike infinity
i^{+}
and an asymptotically flat end (neighborhood of spacelike infinity
i^{0}
), which led to the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

However, this is not sufficient to carry out the unconditional construction of Theorem
3.5
, namely construct a black hole interior exhibiting a spacelike-null singularity, for the following reasons:

•

The uncharged spacetime of Theorem
6.1
has a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}
, but no Cauchy horizon from infinity, i.e.,
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}=\emptyset
. This is due to the absence of charge (
F\equiv 0
).

•

The charged spacetime of Theorem
6.7
has both a spacelike singularity
\mathcal{S}=\{r=0\}
and a null Cauchy horizon from infinity
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}\neq\emptyset
, but a subset of this null Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
is exactly isometric to Reissner–Nordström’s: in particular, this subset is not weakly singular and the scalar field is zero. A crucial aspect of Theorem
3.1
, however, is
mass inflation
at
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, and the fact that the scalar field drives the dynamics and thus must be non-zero in the vicinity of
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, so one requires a different construction.

To construct a global one-ended asymptotically flat spacetime obeying the assumptions of Theorem
3.1
, we must ensure that
\phi
is non-zero near
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
, more precisely that (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are satisfied. Our approach is as follows:

1.

We start with a charged spacetime as in Theorem
6.7
, which we truncate at the regular Reissner–Nordström sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{R}}
. Then, instead of extending the spacetime to exactly Reissner–Nordström to its future as in Theorem
6.7
, we extend it to a dynamical horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
such that

\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v)=\Phi_{H}(v),

(6.108)

where
\Phi_{H}(v)
is an arbitrary
10
10

10

We moreover need to cut-off
\Phi_{H}
near the Reissner–Nordström junction to ensure a smooth transition.
profile satisfying (
6.1
). This is the object of Section
6.4.1
below.

2.

Keeping
\Phi_{H}
arbitrary, but still assuming it satisfies (
6.1
) with
s>\frac{3}{2}
, we prove that one can construct an asymptotically flat black hole, with a (transversally) regular event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
on which that (
6.108
) is satisfied. This step uses spherical symmetry to solve “sideways” and requires the smallness of the black hole charge. This is the object of Sections
6.4.2
–
6.4.6
below.

3.

We show that for an adequate choice of
\Phi_{H}(v)
satisfying (
6.1
) with
s>\frac{3}{2}
, the assumptions (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are satisfied at the Cauchy horizon
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
: more precisely, we require
\Phi_{H}
to satisfy (
6.5
). We relegate the proof of this step, which relies on a refinement of the scattering strategy of
[
40
]
to the next section—Section
6.5
.

The completion of Step
1
and Step
2
in this section will result in the proof of Theorem
6.10
below. As in Theorem
6.1
/Theorem
6.7
, we do not have the freedom to fix the final black hole mass
M
arbitrarily, due to our requirement that the event horizon does not intersect the initial hypersurface (recall Remark
6.1
). Note, in addition, that unlike Theorem
6.7
, the final charge ratio
q
now cannot be chosen a priori; instead, it is only approximately prescribed, up to an arbitrarily small degree of precision
\delta
.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
and
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
, a subset of the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a hypersurface
\Sigma_{L}
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres and such that
b_{\Gamma}
is a first singularity.

Then there exists
q_{L}\in(0,1)
such that for all
q\in(0,q_{L})
,
\varsigma=\pm 1
and
\Phi_{H}(v)
, a
C^{k}
function on
[1,+\infty)
satisfying (
6.1
) for some
s>\frac{3}{2}
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
v_{0}>1
sufficiently large,
M>0
,
q^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that

\begin{split}|q-q^{\prime}|<\delta q,\end{split}

(6.109)

and
C^{k}
solutions
(\mathcal{M},g,\phi)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\neq 0
with the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma
with one end for (

)–(

) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres.

•

The black hole region of

is non-empty with an event horizon

and
\mathcal{H}^{+}
does not intersect
\Sigma

. Moreover, for

\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v)=\Phi_{H}(v),

(6.110)

where
v

iii
).

•

The black hole region converges to a sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström black hole of mass
M
and charge
\varsigma q^{\prime}M_{f}
towards
i^{+}
, in the sense that

\begin{split}&\lim_{p\rightarrow i^{+}}\varpi(p)=M,\\
&\lim_{p\rightarrow i^{+}}Q(p)=\varsigma q^{\prime}M.\end{split}

(6.111)

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
coincides with
\mathcal{M}_{L}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
. Moreover,
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
can be chosen to be in the complement of the causal past of
b_{\Gamma}
.

Similarly to the previous couples Theorem
6.1
/Corollary
6.2
and Theorem
6.7
/Corollary

, we formulate a corollary to Theorem

applying the result where

corresponds to the FLRW spacetime of Proposition

.

.

6.4.1
Construction of spherically symmetric event horizons

We start by constructing the event horizon with “initial conditions” at timelike infinity
i^{+}
, solving the null constraints (system of ODEs).

Proposition 6.12
.

Let
(M,e)
such that
0<|e|<M
,
C^{1}
functions
\phi_{H}(v)
satisfying (
6.5
) for some
s>1
and
\phi_{in}(U)
satisfying (
6.6
), with
\phi_{in}(0)=\phi_{H}(v_{0})
. Then, for sufficiently large
v_{0}
and sufficiently small
U_{S}>0
and imposing the gauge condition (
iii
) on
\mathcal{H}^{+}=\{U=0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
and the gauge (
iv
) on
[0,U_{S}]\times\{v_{0}\}
, there exist unique solutions
(r,\Omega,Q)
of the ODE system in
v
consisting of (
2.4
), (
2.7
), (
2.9
) on
\mathcal{H}^{+}=\{U=0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
and of the ODE system consisting of (
2.6
), (
2.8
) on
[0,U_{S}]\times\{v_{0}\}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to+\infty}r(0,v)=r_{+}(M,e)=M+\sqrt{M^{2}+e^{2}},

(6.116)

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to+\infty}Q(0,v)=e.

(6.117)

Moreover,
\mathcal{H}^{+}
is in the regular region, namely
\partial_{v}r(0,v)\geq 0
for all
v\geq v_{0}
, and in fact
\partial_{v}r(0,v)>0
if
\Phi_{H}
is not identically zero.

Proof.

We already prescribed
\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v)=\phi_{H}(v)
, where
\phi_{H}(v)
satisfies (
6.1
) for some
s>1
. On
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we work with the variables
(r,\lambda,\Omega^{2},Q)
subjected to the following system of ODEs (
2.4
), (
2.7
), (
2.9
) which we re-write, under the gauge condition (
iii
), (
2.37
) as:

\displaystyle\partial_{v}r=\lambda,

(6.118)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}(\frac{\lambda}{\Omega^{2}})=-\frac{r|\partial_{v}\phi_{H}|^{2}}{\Omega^{2}},

(6.119)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}(r\Omega^{2})=\Omega^{2}[1-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}],

(6.120)

\displaystyle\partial_{v}Q=-q_{0}r^{2}\Im(\overline{\phi_{H}}\partial_{v}\phi_{H}).

(6.121)

Let
r_{+}>|e|>0
. Then, we impose initial conditions at
v=+\infty
for this system of ODEs, as such:

\displaystyle\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}r(0,v)=r_{+},

(6.122)

\displaystyle\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\lambda(0,v)=0,

(6.123)

\displaystyle\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}Q(0,v)=e,

(6.124)

together with an initial condition at
v=v_{0}
:

\Omega^{2}(v_{0})=e^{2K_{+}v_{0}},

(6.125)

where (
6.125
) is imposed to respect the gauge compatibility of gauge (
iii
) and gauge (
iv
) at the sphere
(U,v)=(0,v_{0})
. Note that, by (
6.120
), the above conditions implies that

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\partial_{v}\log(r\Omega^{2})(v)=\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})(v)=\frac{1-\frac{e^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}}{r_{+}}:=2K_{+}>0,

(6.126)

Then, we solve backwards the ODE system in
v
with unknown
(r,\log(\Omega^{2}),Q)
and final condition for
\log(\Omega^{2})
determined by (
6.125
)

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\log(\Omega^{2})-2K_{+}v=\log(A_{0}),

(6.127)

where
A_{0}>0
is precisely chosen so that (
6.125
) holds (note indeed that (
6.119
), (
6.120
) are invariant by rescaling
\Omega^{2}
by a constant): this procedure produces a unique solution to the system of ODE with above conditions for
v\in[v_{0},+\infty)
, assuming
v_{0}
is large enough.
Defining
\varpi(v)
through the explicit formula:

1-\frac{2\varpi(v)}{r(v)}+\frac{Q^{2}(v)}{r^{2}(v)}=\lambda(v),

(6.128)

we find that there exists
M>0
such that

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi(v)=M

and

r_{+}=M+\sqrt{M^{2}-e^{2}},\ 2K_{+}=\frac{2}{r_{+}^{2}}[M-\frac{e^{2}}{r_{+}}].

Note that the formula

M=\frac{r_{+}}{2}[1+\frac{e^{2}}{r_{+}^{2}}],

(6.129)

allows to fix
r_{+}
so that the couple
(M,e)
with
0\leq|e|<M
equates its prescribed values.

The analogous construction on
[0,U_{s}]\times\{v_{0}\}
is straightforward, for
U_{s}
small enough.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

In the above proof, it is also possible to take
|e|=M
and relax the condition to
s>\frac{1}{2}
with no additional difficulty. The same is true about the event horizon estimates in Corollary
6.13
that provides further quantitative estimates.

Corollary 6.13
.

On the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}=\{U=0,v\geq v_{0}\}
, the following estimates hold true:

0\leq\lambda(0,v)\lesssim v^{-2s},

(6.130)

0\leq r_{+}-r(0,v)\lesssim v^{1-2s},

(6.131)

|\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})(0,v)-2K_{+}|\lesssim v^{1-2s},

(6.132)

|\partial_{U}\log(\Omega^{2})|(0,v)\lesssim e^{2K_{+}v},

(6.133)

|D_{U}\phi|(0,v)\lesssim e^{2K_{+}v}v^{-s},

(6.134)

|Q(0,v)-e|,\ |\varpi(0,v)-M|\lesssim v^{1-2s}.

(6.135)

Moreover, for any sufficiently small, but fixed
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
(independent of
v_{0}
), we can choose
U_{S}
so that

r(U_{S},v_{0})=r_{+}[1-\delta_{0}],

and the sphere
(U_{S},v_{0})
is trapped.

Proof.

This is the content of Proposition 4.2 and Proposition 4.4 in
[
59
]
but we provide a brief sketch for the reader. Using the fact that
r(0,v)
is bounded and integrating (
2.7
), (
2.16
) gives (
6.135
). Then, integrating (
6.120
) and using (
6.135
) gives (
6.132
).
From (
2.9
) and
\underset{v\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}\lambda(0,v)=0
, we know that
\lambda(0,v)\geq 0
, and using (
6.132
) gives (
6.130
). (
6.133
),(
6.134
) then follow integrating (
2.3
) and (
2.5
) in
v
, respectively.

Regarding the claims on the sphere
(U_{S},v_{0})
, this follows from the fact that
U_{A}(v)e^{2K_{+}v}\lesssim\lambda(0,v)\lesssim v^{-2s}
, where
U_{A}(v)
is defined so that
(U_{A}(v),v)\in\mathcal{A}
(see again Proposition 4.2 and Proposition 4.4 in
[
59
]
).
∎

Now, we address the first step of Theorem
6.10
in constructing the requested spacetime up to its event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
. The corresponding black hole exterior region (including an affine complete null infinity to the future, and an asymptotically flat end) will be constructed in Sections
6.4.2
–
6.4.6
below. Since we do not yet construct an initial hypersurface
\Sigma
that does not intersect
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, we have the freedom to prescribe the final black hole mass (compare with Remark
6.1
), a freedom which we will, however, lose in Theorem
6.10
.

Proposition 6.14
.

[Construction of the black hole interior region].
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geq 2
and
(\mathcal{M}_{L},g_{L},\phi_{L})
, a subset of the MGHD of smooth
spherically symmetric asymptotically flat initial data on a hypersurface
\Sigma_{L}
with one end for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) containing no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped spheres.

Let
q\in(0,1)
. There exists
M_{f}^{inf}(g_{L},q)>0
, such that for all
\varsigma=\pm 1
,
M_{f}>M_{f}^{inf}
and
\Phi_{H}(v)
, a
C^{k}
function on
[1,+\infty)
satisfying (
6.1
) for some
s>1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
a sufficiently small number, there exist
v_{0}>1
sufficiently large,
(M,e)
real numbers such that
0<|e|<M
and

\begin{split}&|M-M_{f}|<\delta M_{f},\\
&|e-\varsigma qM_{f}|<\delta qM_{f},\end{split}

(6.136)

and
C^{k}
solutions
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\neq 0
with the following properties:

•

(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
is a spherically symmetric solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
). Its past boundary
\Sigma\cup\mathcal{H}^{+}
is spherically symmetric, connected, and
\Sigma
is a spacelike hypersurface diffeomorphic to a open ball of center
\Gamma
and
\mathcal{H}^{+}
is a null affine-complete outgoing cone on which
r
is bounded and strictly increasing towards the future.

Moreover,
\Sigma\cup\mathcal{H}^{+}
contain no anti-trapped spheres and no trapped or marginally-trapped spheres.
We denote
i^{+}
the future endpoint of the hypersurface
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

•

\mathcal{H}^{+}
converges to a sub-extremal Reissner–Nordström black hole of mass
M_{f}
and charge
\varsigma qM_{f}
towards
i^{+}
, in the sense that

\begin{split}&\lim_{p\rightarrow i^{+}}\varpi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}(p)=M_{f},\\
&\lim_{p\rightarrow i^{+}}Q_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}(p)=\varsigma qM_{f}.\end{split}

(6.137)

•

There exists an incoming null cone
\underline{C}_{v_{H}}
such that
\mathcal{M}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
coincides with
\mathcal{M}_{L}\cap J^{-}(\underline{C}_{v_{H}})
.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
the spacetime constructed as an application of Theorem
6.7
, whose assumptions are satisfied. We denote
M_{f}
the mass of the Reissner–Nordström black hole, and
e_{f}
its charge, with

e_{f}=\varsigma qM_{f},

and, as in the proof of Theorem
6.7
, we arrange for the trapped sphere
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
(of radius
R_{f}
) to be mildly trapped in the sense that

\frac{2M_{f}}{R_{f}}-1-\frac{e_{f}^{2}}{R_{f}^{2}}>0

is sufficiently small.

Let
v_{0}>1
and
U_{S}>0
such that the conclusion of Proposition
6.12
applies. Let
M
sufficiently close to
M_{f}
,
e
sufficiently close to
e_{f}
, to be chosen later. We choose the
v
-coordinate according to the gauge choice (
iii
) on the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and since the metric is Reissner–Nordström in a broader region, we have

\frac{-4\partial_{u}r}{\Omega^{2}}(u,v)=1,

(6.138)

for all
u\leq u_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
and
v\geq v_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
, where
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}=(u_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}},v_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}})
. With no loss of generality and by taking
v_{0}
larger if necessary, we can assume that
v_{0}>v_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
.

Since
\frac{2M_{f}}{R_{f}}-1-\frac{e_{f}^{2}}{R_{f}^{2}}
is arbitrarily small, in the
U
-gauge (
iv
), we can assume that there exists
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{R}}
, a regular sphere which we normalize to be
(U=0,v=v_{0})
, such that
(U=U_{S},v=v_{0})\in\mathcal{T}
, and
U_{S}
can be chosen as small as desired.

Then, we apply Proposition
6.12
with choice
(M,e)
to the bicharacteristic initial data on
\left([0,U_{S}]\times\{v_{0}\}\right)\cup\left(\{U=0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)\right)
as follows

\begin{split}&\phi(U,v_{0})\equiv 0,\ \varpi(U,v_{0})\equiv M_{f},\ Q(U,v_{0})\equiv\varsigma qM_{f},\\
&\phi(0,v)=\left(1-\chi(v-v_{0})\right)\Phi_{H}(v),\end{split}

(6.139)

where
\chi
a smooth compactly supported function such that
\chi(x)=1
when
0\leq x\leq 1
and
\chi(x)=0
when
x\geq 2
. Note that

|\varpi(0,v_{0})-M|\lesssim v_{0}^{1-2s},\ |Q(0,v_{0})-e|\lesssim v_{0}^{1-2s},

(6.140)

so we can choose
(M,e)
to be
(M_{f},e_{f})+O(v_{0}^{1-2s})
so that

\varpi(0,v_{0})=M_{f},\ Q(0,v_{0})=e_{f}.

(6.141)

Once the null constraints are solved by Proposition
6.12
and Corollary
6.13
, we apply Theorem
1.9
to obtain a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the spacetime rectangle
(U,v)\in[0,U_{S}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
, which is exactly Reissner–Nordström on
[0,U_{S}]\times\{v=v_{0}\}
.

The proof of the proposition is finally completed once we continue the Reissner–Nordström ingoing cone up to
U=U^{\mathcal{T}}_{RN}
and collate the spacetime rectangle obtained as an application of Theorem
1.9
to
\mathbb{S}_{RN}^{\mathcal{T}}
. ∎

6.4.2
Backwards propagation from the event horizon to a constant-
r
curve

We now consider the spacetime of Proposition
6.14
, which is the causal future
\Sigma\cup\mathcal{H}^{+}
, a spherically symmetric solution of (
1.1
)-(
1.4
) with
F\neq 0
. We additionally assume that (
6.5
) holds on
\mathcal{H}^{+}
for some
s>\frac{3}{2}
(note that this is more demanding that the assumption
s>1
from Proposition
6.14
). With no loss of generality, we assume that
\frac{3}{2}<s<2
for convenience of notation. We denote

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}=M>0,\ \lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}Q_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}=e\neq 0,

(6.142)

where we choose
|e|
sufficiently small, possibly depending on
q_{0}
,
M
and
s
: in particular,
|e|<M
. In this section, we begin the construction of an asymptotically flat end that we will “glue” to the past of this spacetime.
Let
\epsilon>0
chosen so that
\epsilon e^{2K_{+}v_{0}}
is sufficiently small. We impose initial data on the ingoing cone
[-\epsilon,0]\times\{v_{0}\}
satisfying (
6.6
). Our first objective is to solve “rightward” in the spacetime rectangle
[-\epsilon,0]\times\{v_{0}\}\cup\{0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
, for
\epsilon
small enough and
v_{0}
large enough. However, in this section, we will only solve up to a timelike curve
\gamma_{R_{0}}
on which
r
converges to a large constant
R_{0}>r_{+}
. The construction of the solution to the future of
\gamma_{R_{0}}
will be completed in the next Section
6.4.3
and Section
6.4.4
.

We recall the gauge condition (
iii
), which we supplement with the following gauge choice for
U
:

\partial_{U}r(U,v_{0})=-e^{2K_{+}(M,e)v_{0}},

(6.143)

corresponding to gauge (
iv
). We will later switch to the different
u
-gauge (
vi
) (see Section
6.4.4
), although we first need to show it is well-defined, since it is a teleological gauge.

Then, we can apply Corollary
6.13
to obtain estimates on the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}=\{0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
. We will establish estimates in the following red-shift region defined as

\mathcal{R}=\{-\epsilon\leq U\leq 0,\ v_{0}\leq v\leq v_{\mathcal{R}}(U)\},\ v_{\mathcal{R}}(U)=v_{0}+[2K_{+}]^{-1}\ln(\frac{\epsilon}{|U|})=v_{0}+u(U)-[2K_{+}]^{-1}\log(\epsilon^{-1}),

(6.144)

where
u(U)
is defined so that
-U=[2K_{+}]^{-1}e^{-2K_{+}u}
and
\Delta=[2K_{+}]^{-1}\ln(\epsilon^{-1})
.

Proposition 6.15
.

For all
(U,v)\in\mathcal{R}
, the following estimates hold:

|\phi|(U,v)+|\partial_{v}\phi|(U,v)\lesssim v^{-s},

(6.145)

|D_{U}\phi|(U,v)\lesssim e^{2K_{+}v}v^{-s},

(6.146)

|\kappa(U,v)-1|\lesssim\epsilon v^{-2s},

(6.147)

|\varpi(U,v)-M|,\ |Q(U,v)-e|\lesssim v^{1-2s},

(6.148)

\bigl|\log(\Omega^{2})(U,v)-2K_{+}(u+v)\bigr|\lesssim|U|e^{2K_{+}v},

(6.149)

|\partial_{U}\log(\Omega^{2})(U,v)|,\ |A_{U}|(U,v)\lesssim e^{2K_{+}v},

(6.150)

|\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})(U,v)-2K(u,v)|\lesssim v^{-2s}.

(6.151)

On the curve
\gamma_{\mathcal{R}}=\{(u,v_{\mathcal{R}}(u)),\ u\geq\Delta\}

\bigl|\iota(u,v_{\mathcal{R}}(u))-1\bigr|\lesssim u^{-s},

(6.152)

\bigl|\partial_{u}\log(\Omega^{2})(u,v_{\mathcal{R}}(u))+2K(u,v_{\mathcal{R}}(u))\bigr|\lesssim u^{-s}.

(6.153)

Proof.

The proof follows entirely from that of Proposition 4.5 in
[
59
]
, but we give a brief sketch of the proof for the reader. We make the following bootstrap assumptions:

|\phi|+|\partial_{v}\phi|\leq 4Cv^{-s},

(6.154)

|D_{U}\phi|\leq De^{2K_{+}v}v^{-s},

(6.155)

|\partial_{U}r|\leq De^{2K_{+}v},

(6.156)

\frac{1}{2}\leq\kappa\leq 1,

(6.157)

|Q-e|\leq 4|e|,

(6.158)

where
C>0
is chosen so that

|\phi|(0,v)+|\partial_{v}\phi|(0,v)\leq Cv^{-s}.

(6.159)

First, we integrate (
6.156
) in
U
to obtain for
v_{0}
large enough and using Corollary
6.13

|r(U,v)-r_{+}|\lesssim|r(0,v)-r_{+}|+|U|e^{2K_{+}v}\lesssim\frac{r_{+}}{10},

(6.160)

where in the last line we have used the fact that
|U|e^{2K_{+}v}\leq\epsilon e^{2K_{+}v_{0}}
in this region. Similarly integrating (
2.15
) and (
2.6
) gives

|\varpi(U,v)-M|,\ |Q(U,v)-e|\lesssim v^{1-2s}.

(6.161)

which improves on (
6.158
) for
v_{0}
large enough. Then integrating (
2.11
) give

|A_{U}|\lesssim e^{2K_{+}v},

hence

|\partial_{U}\phi|\lesssim De^{2K_{+}v}v^{-s}.

We can then integrate to get :

|\phi(U,v)-\phi(0,v)|\lesssim\epsilon e^{2K_{+}v_{0}}v^{-s},

(6.162)

thus (
6.154
) is improved for
\epsilon
small enough. Let
a>0
. We can rewrite (
2.5
) together with (
2.4
) as:

\partial_{v}(e^{av}r\frac{D_{U}\phi}{\nu_{H}})=\left(a-\kappa(2K-rm^{2}|\phi|^{2})\right)e^{av}r\frac{D_{U}\phi}{\nu_{H}}-e^{av}\partial_{v}\phi+\kappa e^{av}rm^{2}\phi.

(6.163)

By the earlier estimates, we get

|K(U,v)-K_{+}|\lesssim\epsilon e^{2K_{+}v_{0}},

and therefore
K(U,v)
is lower bounded by
\frac{3K_{+}}{4}
for small enough
\epsilon
. Choosing say
0<a<\frac{K_{+}}{4}
and integrating (
6.163
) then improves on bootstrap (
6.155
).

Integrating (
2.8
) in
U
using the above estimates then also improves on (
6.157
) for
\epsilon
small enough. The estimates on
\Omega
then easily follow from the integration of (
2.3
); the reader can consult
[
59
]
for details.

(
6.152
) and (
6.153
) are a bit trickier to prove but the argument is identical to that of Proposition 4.6 in
[
59
]
.

∎

Next, we turn to the region where
r
is bounded and away from the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
, i.e., the region
\{v_{\mathcal{R}}(U)\leq v\leq v_{R_{0}}(U),\ u\geq\Delta\}
, where
v_{R_{0}}(u)=R_{0}+u
, where
R_{0}>r_{+}
is a large constant to be determined later. This region is analogous to the no-shift region
\mathcal{N}
in
[
59
]
and the proof of the estimates is very similar, using a Grönwall-like iteration.

Proposition 6.16
.

The following estimates hold:

|\phi|(u,v)+|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{-s}

(6.164)

|D_{u}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{-s},

(6.165)

|\log(\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4})-\log(1-\frac{2M}{r(u,v)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(u,v)})|\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{1-2s},

(6.166)

0\leq 1-\kappa(u,v)\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{-2s},

(6.167)

|1-\iota(u,v)|\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{-s},

(6.168)

|\partial_{u}\log(\Omega^{2})(u,v)+2\frac{M-\frac{e^{2}}{r(u,v)}}{r^{2}(u,v)}|\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{-s},

(6.169)

|\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})(u,v)-2K(u,v)|\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{-2s},

(6.170)

|Q(u,v)-e|\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{1-2s},

(6.171)

|\varpi(u,v)-M|\lesssim_{R_{0}}v^{1-2s}.

(6.172)

Proof.

For the proof, we cut
\mathcal{N}=\{v_{\mathcal{R}}(U)\leq v\leq v_{R_{0}}(U),\ u\geq\Delta\}
into small regions

\mathcal{N}=\overset{N}{\underset{i=1}{\cup}}\mathcal{N}_{i},

where
\mathcal{N}_{i}=\{v_{i-1}(u)\leq v\leq v_{i}(u),\ u\geq\Delta\}
and
v_{0}(u)=v_{\mathcal{R}}(u)
,
v_{N}(u)=v_{R_{0}}(u)
and
v_{i}(u)-v_{i}(u)=\eta_{0}
, a small number. We then prove the result by induction on
i
, making use of a standard bootstrap method and obtaining exponential growth in
N
. The reader interested by the details can consult
[
59
]
, Appendix B which employs the same proof.
∎

6.4.3
Backwards propagation from a constant-
r
curve to a timelike geodesic

We define the timelike curve
\gamma=\{(u,v_{\gamma}(u)),\ v_{\gamma}(u)=\frac{3u}{2},\ u\geq\Delta\}
; note that
\gamma
is not technically a timelike geodesic but it approaches one as
u\rightarrow+\infty
, corresponding to hyperbolic motion on Reisser–Nordström. We will prove estimates in the following spacetime region

\{u\geq\Delta,\ v_{R}(u)\leq v\leq v_{\gamma}(u)\}.

(6.173)

The proof relies on the use of the
r^{p}
-method and takes advantage of the smallness of
e
, similarly to the approach employed in
[
61
]
. However, a major difference is that the well-controlled boundary terms are located on a (timelike) curve
\gamma_{R_{0}}
to the past of the region of integration, and therefore one can only apply for
r^{p}
method for
p<0
, trying to keep
|p|
as small as possible.

Proposition 6.17
.

|\phi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},

(6.174)

r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v),\ |D_{u}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},

(6.175)

r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v),\ |\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},

(6.176)

|\varpi(u,v)-M|,\ |Q(u,v)-e|\lesssim r^{1+\eta}u^{-2s}\lesssim u^{-2s+1+\eta},

(6.177)

|\iota(u,v)-1|\lesssim u^{-s},

(6.178)

|\kappa(u,v)-1|\lesssim\log(u)u^{-2s+1+2\eta},

(6.179)

\bigl|\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4}-(1-\frac{2M}{r(u,v)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(u,v)})\bigr|\lesssim u^{-s},

(6.180)

\bigr|\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})-\frac{2}{r^{2}}[M-\frac{e^{2}}{r}]\bigl|\lesssim u^{-2s+1+2\eta}.

(6.181)

Proof.

We make the following bootstrap asumptions:
(
6.183
),(
6.184
)

|Q|(u,v)\leq 2|e|,

(6.182)

|\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4}-1|\leq\frac{10M}{r},

(6.183)

|\partial_{u}r+1|(u,v)\leq\frac{10M}{r},

(6.184)

|\partial_{v}r-1|(u,v)\leq\frac{10M}{r}.

(6.185)

Let
p>0
. Then, we take advantage of (
2.13
) which we multiply by

r^{-p}\overline{\partial_{v}\psi}
and we take the real-part. Note the identity

r^{-p}\Re(\overline{\partial_{v}\psi}D_{u}\partial_{v}\psi)=r^{-p}\partial_{u}(\frac{|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}}{2})=\partial_{u}(r^{p}\frac{|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}}{2})-pr^{-p-1}[-\nu]|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2},

(6.186)

from which we get, under the bootstrap assumptions (
6.182
)–(
6.185
), and taking
R_{0}
large enough

\bigl|-\partial_{u}(r^{-p}\frac{|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}}{2})+pur^{-p-1}|\nu||\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}\bigr|\leq 2|q_{0}e|r^{-2-p}|\psi||\partial_{v}\psi|.

(6.187)

To control the RHS, we will first prove a Hardy inequality. Let
v_{R_{0}}(u)\leq v\leq v_{\gamma}(u)
and
P>-2
:

\begin{split}&\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-3}|\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}\lambda r^{-P-3}|\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}=-[2+P]^{-1}\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}\partial_{v}(r^{-P-2})|\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}\\
&\leq[2+P]^{-1}R_{0}^{-P}|\phi|^{2}(u,v_{R_{0}}(u))+\frac{2}{2+P}\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-2}|\psi\partial_{v}\psi|dv\lesssim u^{-2s}+\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime},\end{split}

(6.188)

where we have invoked Proposition
6.16
and the bootstrap assumption (
6.185
). In fact, we even proved the stronger estimate and more precise estimates

\begin{split}&\frac{r^{-P}}{2+P}|\phi|^{2}(u,v)+\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-3}|\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}\leq\frac{4}{(P+2)^{2}}\int_{v_{R}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}+C_{P}u^{-2s},\end{split}

(6.189)

and

\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-2}|\psi||\partial_{v}\psi|dv^{\prime}\lesssim[\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-3}|\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{2}{P+2}\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-P-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}dv^{\prime}+C_{P}u^{-2s}.

(6.190)

We combine (
6.190
) with
P=p
with (
6.187
), which gives a coercive estimate as long as
\frac{4|q_{0}e|}{2+p}<p
, i.e.,
p>p_{min}(q_{0}e)=-1+\sqrt{1+4|q_{0}e|^{2}}
, resulting in the following estimate, for such
p

E_{-p}[\psi](u)=\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{+\infty}r^{-p-2}|\psi||\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}+\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{+\infty}r^{-p-3}|\psi|^{2}(u,v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}+\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{+\infty}r^{-p-1}|\partial_{v}\psi|^{2}(u,v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}\lesssim u^{1-2s},

(6.191)

where we invoked Proposition
6.16
to control the boundary term in the past. Let
\eta>0
small: one can choose
|e|
small enough so that
-1+\sqrt{1+4|q_{0}e|^{2}}<2\eta
, hence we will take
p=2\eta
in what follows. Thus, (
6.191
), by (
6.189
), gives in turn

|\phi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}r^{\eta}\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}.

(6.192)

Now, combining (
6.192
) with (
2.13
), we get

|D_{u}\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim r^{-1+\eta}u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}},

(6.193)

which gives the following estimate upon integration in
u
or
v
respectively, and invoking the estimates of Proposition
6.16
(recall that
v
and
u
are comparable in this region):

|D_{u}\psi|(u,v),\ |\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}r^{\eta}\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},

(6.194)

and by (
6.192
), we also have

r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v),\ r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}}r^{\eta}\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}.

(6.195)

It is then easy to integrate (
2.3
), (
2.4
), (
2.6
) using the above estimates to improve on (
6.182
)–(
6.185
) and prove all the remaining estimates (note that to prove (
6.180
), we use (
2.17
) together with (
6.177
), (
6.178
), (
6.179
)).

∎

6.4.4
Backwards propagation from a timelike geodesic to null infinity

In this section, we will prove estimates in the spacetime region to the future on
\gamma
and up to null infinity, namely

\{u\geq\Delta,\ v\geq v_{\gamma}(u)\}.

(6.196)

Proposition 6.18
.

The following estimates hold for all
u\geq\Delta,\ v\geq v_{\gamma}(u)
:

|\psi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{\frac{3}{2}-s+\eta},

(6.197)

r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v),\ |D_{u}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{\frac{1}{2}-s+\eta}

(6.198)

|\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},\ r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-1}u^{\frac{3}{2}-s+\eta},

(6.199)

|\varpi(u,v)-M|,\ |Q(u,v)-e|\lesssim u^{3-2s+2\eta},

(6.200)

|\iota(u,v)-1|\lesssim u^{-s},

(6.201)

|\kappa(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-1}u^{2-2s+2\eta},

(6.202)

\bigl|\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4}-(1-\frac{2M}{r(u,v)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(u,v)})\bigr|\lesssim u^{-2s+1+2\eta}[1+u^{2}v^{-1}],

(6.203)

|\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-2}.

(6.204)

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
6.17
, we make the following bootstrap assumptions:

|Q|(u,v)\leq 2|e|,

(6.205)

|\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4}-1|\leq\frac{10M}{r},

(6.206)

|\partial_{u}r+1|(u,v)\leq\frac{10M}{r},

(6.207)

|\partial_{v}r-1|(u,v)\leq\frac{10M}{r}.

(6.208)

Additionally, we make the bootstrap assumption

r(u,v)\geq\frac{v}{10}.

(6.209)

Then, choosing
|q_{0}e|
small enough, we have

|D_{u}\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\leq 400|q_{0}e|v^{-2}|\psi|(u,v)\leq\eta v^{-2}|\psi|(u,v).

(6.210)

from which we deduce, invoking Proposition
6.17
, there exists
D>0
such that (recall
s<2<\frac{5}{2}
)

|\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}+\eta v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}\sup_{u\leq u^{\prime}\leq u_{\gamma}(v)}[u^{\prime}]^{s-\frac{3}{2}-\eta}|\psi|(u^{\prime},v).

(6.211)

Integrating now in
v
and invoking Proposition
6.17
again gives (recall
s>\frac{3}{2}+\eta
)

|\psi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{3}{2}+\eta}[1+\eta\sup_{u\leq u^{\prime}\leq u_{\gamma}(v)}[u^{\prime}]^{s-\frac{3}{2}-\eta}|\psi|(u^{\prime},v)],

(6.212)

so since
\eta
is small, we close the estimate and improve the bootstrap assumptions, following the same strategy as in Proposition
6.17
.

∎

Before turning to the construction of spatial infinity, we will need to redefine the gauge choice
u
(initially chosen to be (
6.143
)) according to gauge
vi
, i.e., for all
u\geq\Delta
:

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\iota(u,v)=1.

(6.213)

(
6.201
) shows that the new coordinate
u^{\prime}
thus defined is comparable to
u
, and in fact obeys the following estimate as
u\rightarrow+\infty

u^{\prime}=u(1+O(u^{-s})).

(6.214)

We will abuse notation and still use
\iota(u,v)
for
\frac{\partial_{v}r}{\Omega^{2}}
in the new
(u^{\prime},v)
coordinate system and also keep using the letter
u
instead of
u^{\prime}
.

Corollary 6.19
.

Under the new gauge choice (
6.213
), the following estimates hold for
u\geq\Delta
,
v\geq v_{\gamma}(u)
:

|\iota(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-2}u^{-2s+3+2\eta},

(6.215)

\bigl|-\nu(u,v)-(1-\frac{2\varpi(u,v)}{r(u,v)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(u,v)})\bigr|\lesssim r^{-2}u^{-2s+3+2\eta},

(6.216)

|\lambda(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-1},

(6.217)

\bigl|\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4}-(1-\frac{2M}{r(u,v)}+\frac{e^{2}}{r^{2}(u,v)})\bigr|\lesssim u^{-2s+3+2\eta}v^{-1},

(6.218)

|\partial_{u}\log(\Omega^{2})|\lesssim r^{-2}.

(6.219)

Moreover, (
6.198
), (
6.199
), (
6.200
), (
6.202
), (
6.204
) still hold.

Proof.

Redoing the proof of Proposition
6.18
under the new gauge (
6.213
) gives (
6.215
). By (
2.17
), this gives (
6.216
). Then, using (
2.23
), we get

\partial_{u}\log\lambda=\frac{-\nu Q^{2}}{r^{3}}-\frac{-\iota^{-1}+1}{r}[1-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}]-\frac{\nu+1}{r}[1-\frac{Q^{2}}{r^{2}}]=O(r^{-2}),

(6.220)

which, after integrating in
u
, gives (
6.217
). Then, by (
2.17
) combined with (
6.200
), (
6.202
), (
6.215
), we obtain (
6.218
). Integrating (
2.3
), we also obtain (
6.219
).
∎

6.4.5
Backwards propagation from null infinity to spatial infinity

From Proposition
6.17
and Proposition
6.18
, we have obtained a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the spacetime rectangle
[\Delta,+\infty]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
. To construct the asymptotically flat end, we impose initial data on
\mathcal{I}^{+}\cap(-\infty,\Delta]
, where
\mathcal{I}^{+}=\{v=+\infty\}
. We still impose the
u
-gauge (
6.213
) as in the previous section.

We will also assume decay towards spatial initial
i^{0}=\{u=-\infty,v=+\infty\}
in that there exists a constant
D>0
, such that for
u\leq-\Delta
, the following estimate holds:

|\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u)\leq D|u|^{-s+\frac{3}{2}+\eta},\ |D_{u}\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u)\leq D|u|^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}.

(6.221)

First, we fix
\Delta^{\prime}>0
a large constant and construct the solution in the spacetime rectangle
(u,v)\in[-\Delta^{\prime},\Delta]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
. We denote
D^{\prime}(\Delta^{\prime})
such that for all
-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u\leq\Delta
:

|\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u),\ |D_{u}\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u)\leq D^{\prime}.

(6.222)

Lemma 6.20
.

Assuming that
v_{0}
is large enough, depending on
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
, the following estimates hold for all
-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u\leq\Delta
,
v\geq v_{0}
:

|\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4}-1|,\ |\partial_{v}r(u,v)-1|,\ |\partial_{u}r(u,v)+1|\lesssim v^{-1}.

(6.223)

|\psi|(u,v),\ |D_{u}\psi|(u,v),\ r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v),\ |Q|(u,v),\ |\varpi|(u,v)\leq C.

(6.224)

|\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},\ r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-1},

(6.225)

|\partial_{u}\log(\Omega^{2})|(u,v),\ |\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-2},

(6.226)

|\kappa(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-1},

(6.227)

|\iota(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-2}.

(6.228)

Proof.

We make the bootstrap assumptions:

\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4},\ \partial_{v}r(u,v),\ -\partial_{u}r(u,v)\leq 2,

(6.229)

|\psi|(u,v)\leq 2D^{\prime},

(6.230)

|Q|(u,v)\leq C(D^{\prime},e,\Delta),

(6.231)

r(u,v)\geq\frac{v}{10}.

(6.232)

Then, (
2.13
) with (
6.229
), (
6.230
), (
6.231
) gives

|D_{u}\partial_{v}\psi|\lesssim v^{-2},

(6.233)

which upon integrating in
u
and invoking Corollary
6.19
, gives

|\partial_{v}\psi(u,v)|\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}+v^{-2}\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},

(6.234)

hence, integrating in
v
gives

|\psi(u,v)-\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u)|\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{3}{2}+\eta},

(6.235)

which is enough to improve (
6.230
) for
v_{0}
large enough. It is then easy to derive the rest of the estimates and improve the bootstrap assumptions.
∎

Finally, we turn to the propagation towards spacelike infinity
i^{0}=\{u=-\infty,\ v=+\infty\}
.

Proposition 6.21
.

There exists a large constant
D>0
such that for all
u\leq-\Delta^{\prime}
,
v\geq D[-\Delta^{\prime}-u]+v_{0}
, the following estimates hold:

|\frac{\Omega^{2}}{4}(u,v)-1|,\ |\partial_{v}r(u,v)-1|,\ |\partial_{u}r(u,v)+1|\lesssim v^{-1},

(6.236)

|Q|(u,v),\ |\varpi|(u,v)\leq C,

(6.237)

|\psi|(u,v)\lesssim|u|^{-s+\frac{3}{2}+\eta},

(6.238)

|D_{u}\psi|(u,v),\ r|D_{u}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim u^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},

(6.239)

|\partial_{v}\psi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},\ r|\partial_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-1}|u|^{-s+\frac{3}{2}+\eta},

(6.240)

|\partial_{u}\log(\Omega^{2})|(u,v),\ |\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-2},

(6.241)

|\kappa(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-1},

(6.242)

|\iota(u,v)-1|\lesssim v^{-2},

(6.243)

|\partial_{v}\lambda|(u,v),\ |\partial_{u}\lambda|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-2},

(6.244)

|\partial_{v}\nu|(u,v),\ |\partial_{u}\nu|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-2}.

(6.245)

Proof.

We make the following bootstrap assumptions

\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{\Omega^{2}(u,v)}{4},\ \partial_{v}r(u,v),\ -\partial_{u}r(u,v)\leq 2,

(6.246)

|\psi|(u,v)\leq 4\sup_{-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u}|\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u^{\prime}),

(6.247)

|Q|(u,v)\leq C,

(6.248)

r(u,v)\geq\frac{v}{10}.

(6.249)

We get, by (
2.13
)

|D_{u}\partial_{v}\psi|\leq Cv^{-2}\sup_{-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u}|\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u^{\prime}),

(6.250)

hence, integrating in
u
, invoking Lemma
6.20

|\partial_{v}\psi|\leq D[v^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta}+v^{-2}[-\Delta^{\prime}-u]\sup_{-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u}|\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u^{\prime})].

(6.251)

Thus, now integrating in
v
:

|\psi(u,v)-\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u)|\leq D^{\prime}v^{-s+\frac{3}{2}+\eta}+Dv^{-1}[-\Delta^{\prime}-u]\sup_{-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u^{\prime}\leq u}|\psi_{|\mathcal{I}^{+}}|(u^{\prime}).

(6.252)

To improve on (
6.247
), we just need that
v_{0}
large enough and moreover

v\geq D[-\Delta^{\prime}-u]+v_{0}.

(6.253)

The other bootstrap assumptions improvements and other claimed estimates are then straightforward to derive, except perhaps (
6.244
), (
6.245
), which require a bit more explanation. Invoking (
2.9
) under the form
\partial_{v}\log(\lambda)-\partial_{v}\log(\Omega^{2})=\frac{-r|\partial_{v}\phi|^{2}}{\lambda}
, together with (
6.240
), (
6.241
) shows that

|\partial_{v}\log(\lambda)|\lesssim v^{-2}.

(6.254)

Similarly, invoking (
2.8
) together with (
6.239
), (
6.241
) shows that

|\partial_{u}\log(\nu)|\lesssim v^{-2}.

(6.255)

Now, invoking (
2.4
) combined with (
6.236
) immediately gives

|\partial_{u}\lambda|(u,v),\ |\partial_{v}\nu|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-2},

(6.256)

which completes the proof of (
6.244
), (
6.245
).
∎

6.4.6
Construction of the charged one-ended black hole

We are now ready to complete the proof of Theorem
6.10
and Corollary
6.11
.

Proof.

We start with
(\mathcal{M},g,F,\phi)
solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with
F\not 0
, corresponding to the spacetime of Proposition
6.14
. The gauge is chosen to be gauge (
iii
) and gauge
vi
(which we recall comes from (
6.213
), which is a teleological choice).

A.

[Existence up to a constant-
r
timelike curve near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
]. We pose consider the characteristic initial value problem for (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with data on
[-\epsilon,0]\times\{v_{0}\}\cup\{0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
recalling that
v_{0}>1
is sufficiently large. Then, we invoke Proposition
6.15
and Proposition
6.16
with characteristic initial data on
[-\epsilon,0]\times\{v_{0}\}\cup\{0\}\times[v_{0},+\infty)
to solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the region
\{-\epsilon\leq U\leq 0,\ v_{0}\leq v\leq v_{R_{0}}(u)\}
, where we recall
v_{R_{0}}(u)
is defined so that
v_{R_{0}}(u)+u=R_{0}
, where
R_{0}
is a large constant.

B.

[Existence up to a far-away timelike curve
\gamma
near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
]. We invoke Proposition
6.17
to solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the region
\{u\geq\Delta,\ v_{R_{0}}(u)\leq v\leq v_{\gamma}(u)\}
, where we recall
v_{\gamma}(u)=\frac{3u}{2}
, and
u=[2K_{+}]^{-1}\ln(|U|^{-1})
, with
\Delta=[2K_{+}]^{-1}\ln(\epsilon^{-1})
.

C.

[Existence up to null infinity
\mathcal{I}^{+}
near
\mathcal{H}^{+}
]. We invoke Proposition
6.18
and Corollary
6.19
to solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the region
\{u\geq\Delta,\ v\geq v_{\gamma}(u)\}
(
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{H}}
in Figure
17
).

D.

[Existence up to spacelike infinity
i^{0}
]. We invoke Lemma
6.20
to solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the region
\{-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u\leq\Delta,\ v\geq v_{0}\}
(
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{I}}
in Figure
17
)., and subsequently Proposition
6.21
to solve (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the region
\{u\leq-\Delta^{\prime},\ v\geq v_{0}+D[-\Delta^{\prime}-u]\}
, for some (sufficiently large) constant
D>0
(
\mathcal{D}_{i^{0}}
in Figure
17
).

Combining Steps
A
–
D
gives a solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) in the following spacetime region (Figure
17
)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}^{\prime}:=R_{\mathcal{H}}\cup R_{\mathcal{I}}\cup\mathcal{D}_{i^{0}},\\
&R_{\mathcal{H}}:=\{-\epsilon\leq U(u)\leq 0,\ v\geq v_{0}\},\\
&R_{\mathcal{I}}:=\{-\Delta^{\prime}\leq u\leq\Delta,\ v\geq v_{0}\},\\
&\mathcal{D}_{i^{0}}:=\{u\leq-\Delta^{\prime},\ v\geq v_{0}+D[-\Delta^{\prime}-u]\}.\end{split}

(6.257)

Figure 17:
Construction of an asymptotically flat end corresponding to
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, the right of the event horizon in the Penrose diagram, here labeled as
\{U=0\}
as part of the proof of Theorem
6.10
.

Then, one can glue
\mathcal{M}
to
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
to obtain a global spacetime. Finally, one can proceed as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
, and construct a spacelike hypersurface
\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with the following properties (see Figure
18
):

1.

\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}
does not intersect with the event horizon
\mathcal{H}^{+}
.

2.

\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}
terminates to the left at the center
\Gamma
.

3.

In a neighborhood of
\Gamma
, the induced data on
\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}
coincides with that of
\Sigma
from Proposition
6.14
.

4.

\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}
does not have any (marginally) trapped sphere, i.e.,
\lambda_{|\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}}>0
.

5.

\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}
does not have any (marginally) antitrapped sphere, i.e.,
\nu_{|\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}}<0
.

6.

For some
\Delta^{\prime\prime}>\Delta^{\prime}
,
\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}\cap\{u\leq-\Delta^{\prime\prime}\}
coincides with
\{v=D[-\Delta^{\prime}+u]+v_{0},\ u\leq-\Delta^{\prime\prime}\}
.

Figure 18:
Asymptotically flat one-ended black hole construction in the proof of Theorem
6.10
.

Moreover, by Theorem
1.9
, the MGHD terminal boundary admits a non-empty null component
\mathcal{CH}_{i^{+}}
on which
r
extends to a non-zero function.
Finally, we need to check that the spacetime is asymptotically flat in the sense of Definition
2.1
. By definition,
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\cap J^{+}(\Sigma_{0}^{\prime})
is the MGHD of initial data
(r,f,h,l,\phi,\dot{\phi})
corresponding to (
2.42
), (
2.43
), (
2.44
), where
\vec{n}
is the future-directed unit normal to
\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}
defined as such,

\begin{split}&f(\rho):=\Omega(\rho),\ l(\rho):=\frac{r(\rho)}{\Omega(\rho)}[\lambda(\rho)+\nu(\rho)],\\
&h(\rho)=\frac{r(\rho)l^{2}(\rho)}{\lambda(\rho)-\nu(\rho)}\left[\frac{\partial_{\rho}l(\rho)}{r^{2}(\rho)}-\frac{l(\rho)[\lambda(\rho)-\nu(\rho)]}{r^{3}(\rho)}+\frac{\theta^{2}(\rho)-\xi^{2}(\rho)}{f(\rho)r(\rho)}\right].\end{split}

(6.258)

where
\rho=u^{\Gamma}_{|\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}}-u_{|\Sigma_{0}^{\prime}}
. Then, by Proposition
6.21
, the following estimates hold as
\rho\rightarrow+\infty
:

\begin{split}&|r(\rho)-\rho|\lesssim\log(\rho),\ |\partial_{\rho}r(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-1},\ |\partial_{\rho}^{2}r(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-2},\\
&|f(\rho)-1|\lesssim\rho^{-1},\ |\partial_{\rho}f(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-2},\\
&|l(\rho)|\lesssim 1,\ |\partial_{\rho}l(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-1},\\
&|h(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-2},\\
&|\phi(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-s+\frac{1}{2}+\eta},\ |\dot{\phi}(\rho)|\lesssim\rho^{-s-\frac{1}{2}+\eta},\end{split}

(6.259)

therefore the spacetime is indeed asymptotically flat and Theorem
6.10
is proved. Corollary
6.11
is then obtained as an immediate application of Theorem
6.10
, the same way we proved Corollary
6.2
.

∎

6.5
Nonlinear scattering theory in the black hole interior

In this section, we turn to the proof of finer asymptotics inside the black hole, which are necessary to apply Theorem
3.1
. Ultimately, we will combine Theorem
6.1
and Theorem
6.7
, applying the new scattering result to a profile
\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}
obeying the asymptotics (
6.5
) for
s>\frac{3}{2}
, and in the weakly-charged case
|e|\ll M
of Theorem
6.7
. However, for now we try to remain as general as possible. Let
0<|e|<M
and
\Phi(v)
a
C^{1}
function. We assume

\phi_{|\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v)=\Phi_{H}(v)e^{-iq_{0}\omega_{res}v},

(6.260)

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}\varpi(0,v)=M,\ \lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}Q(0,v)=e,\ \lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}r(0,v)=r_{+}(M,e):=M+\sqrt{M^{2}-e^{2}}.

(6.261)

|\phi|(U,v_{0}),|D_{U}\phi|(U,v_{0})\leq D.

(6.262)

Our nonlinear scattering theorem, instrumental to the proof of Theorem
3.5
, is stated as follows.

Theorem 6.22
.

Let
\Phi(v)
be a
C^{1}
function and
s>1
. Assume that

\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}}\ (1+|v|)^{s}|\Phi_{H}|(v)<+\infty,\ \sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}}\ (1+|v|)^{s}|\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}|(v)<+\infty,\ \partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),\ \partial_{v}^{3}\Phi_{H}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}).

(6.263)

We consider the solution of (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) on the rectangle
[0,U_{s}]\times[v_{0},+\infty)
. Then, there exists
\Delta
large enough so that for all
u\leq-\Delta
, and
v
sufficiently large depending on
u
, there exists
\theta(u)
such that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
:

\bigl|e^{-iq_{0}\int_{v}^{+\infty}A_{v}(u,v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}+i\theta(u)}D_{v}\psi(u,v)+i\frac{r_{+}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})\Phi_{H}(v)\bigr|\lesssim|\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}(v)|+(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(1+\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)+v^{1-2s},

(6.264)

\bigl||D_{v}^{2}\psi|(u,v)-\frac{r_{+}|\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})|}{\sqrt{2\pi}}|\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}|(v)\bigr|\lesssim|\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}(v)|+(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(1+\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{3}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)+v^{-2s},

(6.265)

\bigl||D_{u}\psi|(u,v)-\frac{r_{+}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}|\mathfrak{r}|(\omega_{res})|\Phi_{H}|(|u|)\bigr|\lesssim|\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}(|u|)|+(1+|u|)^{-N}\left(1+\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)+|u|^{-2s},

(6.266)

where
\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})\in\mathbb{C}-\{0\}
,
\mathfrak{r}(\omega_{res})\in\mathbb{C}-\{0\}
are constants only depending on
M
,
e
and
q_{0}
, and
|\mathfrak{t}|(\omega_{res})=|\mathfrak{r}|(\omega_{res})
.

Then, we apply Theorem
6.22
to profiles satisfying (
6.5
), as explained in the beginning of the section.

Corollary 6.23
.

Let us note
t(\omega_{res})=|t|(\omega_{res})e^{i\theta(\omega_{res})}
.
Under the assumptions of Theorem
6.22
, assume that there exists
\delta>0
such that for all integers
0\leq k\leq 3
:

\begin{split}&|\partial_{v}^{k}\Phi_{H}|(v)\lesssim v^{-s-k},\\
&|\Phi_{H}|(v)\gtrsim v^{-s},\\
&\bigl|\Im(ie^{i\theta(\omega_{res})}\Phi_{H}(v))\bigr|\lesssim v^{-s-\delta}.\end{split}

(6.267)

Then, there exists a large constant
\Delta>0
such that for all
u\leq-\Delta
, and
v
sufficiently large (depending on
u
)

\begin{split}&v^{-s}\lesssim|D_{v}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s},\\
&|D_{v}^{2}\phi|(u,v)\lesssim v^{-s-1}.\end{split}

(6.268)

\begin{split}&\bigl|\Im(e^{-iq_{0}\int_{v}^{+\infty}A_{v}(u,v^{\prime})dv^{\prime}+i\theta(u)}D_{v}\psi(u,v))\bigr|\lesssim v^{-s-\delta^{\prime}},\end{split}

(6.269)

v^{-2s}\lesssim-\partial_{v}r\lesssim v^{-2s},

(6.270)

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}|D_{u}\phi|(u,v)\gtrsim|u|^{-s},

(6.271)

\lim_{v\rightarrow+\infty}|\partial_{u}r|(u,v)>0,

(6.272)

where
\delta^{\prime}=\min\{\delta,s-1\}
. In particular, there exists
u_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that (
3.6
)–(
3.8
) are satisfied.

Remark 6.6
.

We note that there exist examples of functions
\Phi_{H}
satisfying (
6.267
), such as
\Phi_{H}(v)=ie^{-i\theta(\omega_{res})}v^{-s}
(where
\delta=\infty
,
\delta^{\prime}=s-1
in this example).

6.5.1
Recalling previous linear scattering results in the black hole interior from
[
40
]

We place ourselves in the framework of Kehle’s and the author’s previous work
[
40
]
, Section 5, which concerns the linear theory of charged scalar fields on a
fixed
subextremal Reissner–Nordström interior (
1.11
) with the subextremal parameters
0<|e|<M
. We will use the electromagnetic gauge choice, recalling
r_{\pm}(M,e)=M\pm\sqrt{M^{2}-e^{2}}
:

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{RN}=\left(\frac{e}{r}-\frac{e}{r_{+}}\right)dt=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{e}{r}-\frac{e}{r_{+}}\right)dv-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{e}{r}-\frac{e}{r_{+}}\right)du

(6.273)

which satisfies
F_{RN}=\d{A}^{\prime}_{RN}
for

\displaystyle F_{RN}=\frac{e}{2r^{2}}\Omega^{2}_{RN}du\wedge dv.

(6.274)

We now consider solutions
\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
of the charged scalar field equation (
1.4
) with
g=g_{RN}

(\nabla_{\mu}+iq_{0}(A^{\prime}_{RN})_{\mu})(\nabla^{\mu}+iq_{0}(A^{\prime}_{RN})^{\mu})\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}=0,

(6.275)

We also define

\displaystyle\omega_{r}=\frac{q_{0}e}{r},\ \omega_{+}=\frac{q_{0}e}{r_{+}},\ \omega_{-}=\frac{q_{0}e}{r_{-}},\ \omega_{res}=\omega_{-}-\omega_{+}.

(6.276)

Then, we define the
t
-Fourier transform of
\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}](r,\omega)=\hat{\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(r,t)e^{i\omega t}dt,\ u(\omega,r^{\ast}):=r(r^{\ast})\mathcal{F}[\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}](r(r^{\ast}),\omega)

(6.277)

and since
\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
solves (
6.275
),
u
solves the following ODE:

-u^{\prime\prime}-(\omega-(\omega_{r}-\omega_{+}))^{2}u+Vu=0,

(6.278)

where

\displaystyle V=-\Omega^{2}_{RN}(r)\left(\frac{2M}{r^{3}}-\frac{2e^{2}}{r^{4}}\right).

(6.279)

We also define

r^{*}(u,v)=\frac{v+u}{2}.

(6.280)

The radial ODE (
6.278
) admits the following two fundamental pairs of solution associated to the event horizon (
r^{\ast}\to-\infty
) and the Cauchy horizon (
r^{\ast}\to+\infty
).

Definition 6.1
.

Let
u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}
,
u_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}
,
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}
and
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}
be the unique smooth solutions to (
6.278
) satisfying

\displaystyle u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}(r^{\ast})=e^{-i\omega r^{\ast}}+O(\Omega^{2}_{RN})\text{ as }r^{\ast}\to-\infty

(6.281)

\displaystyle u_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}(r^{\ast})=e^{i\omega r^{\ast}}+O(\Omega^{2}_{RN})\text{ as }r^{\ast}\to-\infty

(6.282)

\displaystyle u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(r^{\ast})=e^{i(\omega-\omega_{res})r^{\ast}}+O(\Omega^{2}_{RN})\text{ as }r^{\ast}\to+\infty

(6.283)

\displaystyle u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}(r^{\ast})=e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{res})r^{\ast}}+O(\Omega^{2}_{RN})\text{ as }r^{\ast}\to+\infty

(6.284)

for
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
. The pairs
(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{H}_{L}})
and (
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}
) span the solution space of (
6.278
) for
\omega\in\mathbb{R}-\{0\}
and
\omega\in\mathbb{R}-\{\omega_{res}\}
, respectively.

Using the fact that the Wronskian

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}(f,g):=fg^{\prime}-f^{\prime}g

(6.285)

of two solution of (
6.278
) is independent of
r^{\ast}
, we define the transmission and reflection coefficients
\mathfrak{T}(\omega)
and
\mathfrak{R}(\omega)
as follows.

Definition 6.2
.

For
\omega\in\mathbb{R}-\{\omega_{res}\}
, we define the transmission and reflection coefficients
\mathfrak{T}
and
\mathfrak{R}
as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}(\omega):=\frac{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}})}{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}})}=\frac{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}})}{2i(\omega-\omega_{res})}

(6.286)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}(\omega):=\frac{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}})}{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}})}=\frac{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}})}{-2i(\omega-\omega_{res})},

(6.287)

where
u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}
,
u_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}
,
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}
and
u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}
are defined in
6.1
. Indeed, this allows us to write

\displaystyle u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}=\mathfrak{T}u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}+\mathfrak{R}u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}

(6.288)

for
\omega\in\mathbb{R}-\{\omega_{res}\}
.
Moreover, we define the normalized transmission and reflection coefficients as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}(\omega)=(\omega-\omega_{res})\mathfrak{T}(\omega)=\frac{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}})}{2i},

(6.289)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{r}(\omega)=(\omega-\omega_{res})\mathfrak{R}(\omega)=\frac{\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}})}{-2i},

(6.290)

which manifestly satisfy

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})=-\mathfrak{r}(\omega_{res})\neq 0.

(6.291)

We will also define the re-normalized functions

Definition 6.3
.

We define

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}(r^{\ast},\omega):=e^{i\omega r^{\ast}}u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}(r^{\ast},\omega),

(6.292)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}(r^{\ast},\omega):=e^{-i\omega r^{\ast}}u_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}(r^{\ast},\omega),

(6.293)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(r^{\ast},\omega):=e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{res})r^{\ast}}u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(r^{\ast},\omega),

(6.294)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}(r^{\ast},\omega):=e^{i(\omega-\omega_{res})r^{\ast}}u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}(r^{\ast},\omega).

(6.295)

The following result is extracted from
[
40
]
and will be used in the subsequent sections.

Proposition 6.24

(
[
40
]
, Theorem V)
.

\partial_{v}\left(re^{i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)=-i\frac{r_{+}e^{i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}[(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}e^{i\omega_{res}\cdot}](\omega)\mathfrak{t}(\omega+\omega_{res})e^{-i\omega v}d\omega+\Phi_{error}(u,v),

(6.296)

where
\Phi_{error}(u,v)
obeys the following estimates for any fixed
0<\alpha<2
and every
(u,v)
such that
v+u\geq 2

|\Phi_{error}|(u,v),\ |\partial_{v}\Phi_{error|}(u,v)\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|\partial_{v}(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|^{2}(v^{\prime})+|(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|^{2}(v^{\prime})]dv\right)\Omega_{RN}^{2-\alpha}(u,v)

(6.297)

and where
\omega\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}(\omega)
is analytic and we have

\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})\neq 0,\ |\mathfrak{t}|(\omega),\ |\partial_{\omega}\mathfrak{t}|(\omega)\lesssim 1+|\omega|.

(6.298)

We then record a representation formula for the ingoing derivative which is not strictly speaking stated in
[
40
]
but immediately follows from the techniques there. The reflection coefficient estimates are in
[
40
]
, Lemma 5.9.

Proposition 6.25
.

The following estimates hold:

\partial_{u}\left(re^{-i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)=i\frac{r_{+}e^{-i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}[(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}e^{i\omega_{res}\cdot}](\omega)\mathfrak{r}(\omega+\omega_{res})e^{i\omega u}d\omega+\tilde{\Phi}_{error}(u,v),

(6.299)

where
\tilde{\Phi}_{error}(u,v)
obeys the following estimates for any fixed
0<\alpha<2
and every
(u,v)
such that
v+u\geq 2

|\tilde{\Phi}_{error}|(u,v)\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|\partial_{v}(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|^{2}(v^{\prime})+|(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}|^{2}(v^{\prime})]dv\right)\Omega_{RN}^{2-\alpha}(u,v)

(6.300)

and where
\omega\rightarrow\mathfrak{r}(\omega)
is analytic and we have

\mathfrak{r}(\omega_{res})\neq 0,\ |\mathfrak{r}|(\omega),\ |\partial_{\omega}\mathfrak{r}|(\omega)\lesssim 1+|\omega|.

(6.301)

6.5.2
New refined linear scattering results

In this section, we remain in the framework of Section
6.5.1
but we provide more refined estimates on the transmission coefficient
\mathfrak{t}
than what is already available in
[
40
]
. These refinements will end up being necessary to prove Theorem
6.22
.

Lemma 6.26
.

For all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\mathfrak{t}|(\omega)\lesssim_{N}1+|\omega|.

(6.302)

Proof.

This lemma can be viewed as a refinement of Lemma 5.9 in
[
40
]
, which itself is based on Lemma 5.7 in
[
40
]
. Recall, for instance, that
\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}
solves the Volterra integral equation (equation (5.77) in
[
40
]
)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(r^{\ast},\omega)=1+\int_{r^{\ast}}^{+\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{\sin[(\omega-\omega_{res})(r^{\ast}-y)]}{\omega-\omega_{res}}e^{-i(\omega-\omega_{res})(r^{\ast}-y)}

(6.303)

\displaystyle\left[V(y)-(\omega_{-}-\omega_{r(y)})(2\omega+2\omega_{+}-\omega_{-}-\omega_{r(y)})\right]\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(\omega,y)dy.

(6.304)

Then, taking
\partial_{\omega}^{N}
derivatives and using standard Volterra estimates as in
[
40
]
generalizes the result into

\begin{split}&|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(r^{\ast},\omega)|\lesssim\Omega^{2}_{RN}(r^{*}),\\
&|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\partial_{r^{*}}\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}(r^{\ast},\omega)|\lesssim\Omega^{2}_{RN}(r^{*})(1+|\omega|),\end{split}

(6.305)

for
r^{*}\geq 0
, and the analogous estimate replacing
\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}}
by
\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{L}}}
. Moreover, we also get, for
r^{*}\leq 0
:

\begin{split}&|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}(r^{\ast},\omega)|\lesssim\Omega^{2}_{RN}(r^{*}),\\
&|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\partial_{r^{*}}\tilde{u}_{\mathcal{H}_{R}}(r^{\ast},\omega)|\lesssim\Omega^{2}_{RN}(r^{*})(1+|\omega|).\end{split}

(6.306)

Now, as in
[
40
]
, we argue evaluating the Wronskian at
r^{*}=0
, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\mathfrak{t}|\lesssim|\partial_{\omega}^{N}\mathfrak{W}(u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}})|\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{N}|\mathfrak{W}(\partial_{\omega}^{k}u_{\mathcal{H}_{R}},\partial_{\omega}^{N-k}u_{\mathcal{CH}_{\mathcal{R}}})|(r^{\ast}=0)\lesssim 1+|\omega|,

(6.307)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Then, in the next step, we take advantage of Lemma
6.26
to prove a refinement of Proposition
6.24
:

Proposition 6.27
.

The following estimates hold true: for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
:

\bigl|\partial_{v}\left(re^{i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)+i\frac{r_{+}e^{i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})\Phi_{H}(v)+\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}(\omega_{res})\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}(v)\right)\bigr|\lesssim(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{2}}\|\right),

(6.308)

\bigl|\partial_{v}^{2}\left(re^{i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)-\frac{r_{+}e^{i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}(v)+\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}(\omega_{res})\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}(v)\right)\bigl|\lesssim(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{3}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{v}^{3}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{2}}\|\right),

(6.309)

\begin{split}&\bigl|\partial_{u}\left(re^{-i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)-i\frac{r_{+}e^{-i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\mathfrak{r}(\omega_{res})\Phi_{H}(v)+\mathfrak{r}^{\prime}(\omega_{res})\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}(v)\right)\bigr|\\
&\lesssim(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{2}}\|\right).\end{split}

(6.310)

Proof.

This proposition builds up on the claims of Theorem V in
[
40
]
and essentially follows from its proof. We nonetheless will give some rapid details on how to obtain the claimed refinements on the estimates of Theorem V in
[
40
]
. First, Statement C in Theorem V in
[
40
]
gives

\partial_{v}\left(re^{i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)=-i\frac{r_{+}e^{i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}[(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}e^{i\omega_{res}\cdot}](\omega)\mathfrak{t}(\omega+\omega_{res})e^{-i\omega v}d\omega+\Phi_{error}(u,v),

(6.311)

where

|\Phi_{error}|(u,v)\lesssim\Omega_{RN}(u,v).

Then, we can write, using Lemma
6.26
to obtain an improvement of
[
40
]
, equation (5.182): for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{t}(\omega+\omega_{res})=\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})+\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}(\omega_{res})\omega+\omega^{2}\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}(\omega),\\
&|\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}|(\omega),\ |\partial_{\omega}^{N}\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}|(\omega)\lesssim(1+|\omega|)^{-1},\end{split}

(6.312)

from which we deduce, by Plancherel Theorem, that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
:

(1+|v|)^{N}\mathcal{F}[\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}]\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{v}),

(6.313)

hence

\mathcal{F}[\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}]\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{v}).

(6.314)

The conclusion of this becomes, defining
\Phi_{H}(v):=(\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime})_{\mathcal{H}^{+}}(v)e^{i\omega_{res}v}
:

\begin{split}&\partial_{v}\left(re^{i\frac{\omega_{res}v}{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v)\right)=-i\frac{r_{+}e^{i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}[\Phi_{H}](\omega)\mathfrak{t}(\omega+\omega_{res})e^{-i\omega v}d\omega+\Phi_{error}(u,v)\\
&=-i\frac{r_{+}e^{i\frac{\omega_{res}u}{2}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\mathfrak{t}(\omega_{res})\Phi_{H}(v)+\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}(\omega_{res})\partial_{v}\Phi_{H}(v)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}[\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}](\omega)\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}(\omega)e^{-i\omega v}d\omega\right)+\Phi_{error}(u,v).\end{split}

(6.315)

Then, we can use (
6.312
), (
6.313
), (
6.314
) to estimate the expression as such to write (see equation (5.191) in
[
40
]
, where the same argument is used):

\begin{split}&\bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}[\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}](\omega)\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}(\omega)e^{-i\omega v}d\omega\bigl|\\
\lesssim&(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}]\|_{L^{1}}+\|\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{2}}\|(1+|v|)^{N}\mathcal{F}[\tilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\omega_{res}}]\|_{L^{2}}\right)\\
\lesssim&(1+|v|)^{-N}\left(\|(1+|v|)^{N}\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{v}^{2}\Phi_{H}\|_{L^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(6.316)

Then, equation (5.178) in
[
40
]
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{2}(e^{i\omega_{res}r^{\ast}}r\phi_{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(u,v))=
